| ![DxNoteCaution.png](data:image/png;base64...) | *CAUTION*  When a progress bar is indicating that the system is processing a large number of points, a message might appear, requesting that you "Please wait." Any additional action performed before the message disappears will cause the system to freeze. |

## Modes of Operation

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Modes\_of\_Operation\_EN.xml

The CARTO™ 3 System has two modes of operation:

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Acquisition Mode | Sometimes referred to as Real-Time or Study mode. Acquisition and all other functions are performed in this mode. |
| Review Mode | This mode is used to review saved studies and enables their limited modification. Acquisition is disabled in this mode. Only a workstation and its peripherals are required in this mode. If there is an interruption in the progress of a study, this mode enables continuation of the study. |

These modes are accessed from the Opening Screen.

### Switching on the CARTO™ 3 System

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/system\_startup\_c3\_v7p3\_EN.xml

Before starting a clinical procedure, prepare the system according to the guidelines in the System Hardware chapter.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Place the Location Pad in its holder under the patient table. |
| 2. | Switch on the PIU Power Supply. |
| 3. | Switch on the Workstation. |

You can begin working in the CARTO™ 3 System when the Opening Screen appears (see [Figure 64: "CARTO™ 3 System Opening Screen"](#target9000046)).

![opening_screen_buttonsOnly_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 64: CARTO™ 3 System Opening Screen

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  The System provides options for HIPAA compliance through the Secure Login feature. If the Secure Login feature is enabled on the CARTO™ 3 System, you must enter a username and password to start. Your username and password are supplied by your hospital's UAC Manager. |

## System Status

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/system\_status\_C3\_v4-3\_EN.xml

During a study, the System Status panel at the upper right of the main screen displays the current state of the system. This includes current date and time, connectivity with other systems, and error or alert messages.

A progress bar indicates the status of the following processes: catheter connection, PIU to workstation connection, and catheter visualization initialization. Error or alert messages may also appear during these processes.

![status_bar_catheterIndication_error_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 65: System Status Panel with Catheter Connection Indication

The System Status icon indicates whether there is a message requiring your attention. If there is, the message number is displayed near the icon.

Table 38: System Status Icons

| Icon | Status | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ![normal_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Normal | The system is operating in standard working order. |
| ![info_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Info | These messages provide information about the current status, but they do not interrupt the course of the procedure. |
| ![alert_icon_v4-2.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Alert | These messages report that something in the system requires attention. Alerts may require an action, but they do not interrupt the course of the procedure. |
| ![error_icon_v4.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Error | These messages report an error state that limits the functionality of the system. You can continue using the system, but acquisition will be disabled and other functions might not be fully operational until you correct the error. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If a Patch Detached error message appears and a gray-striped patch appears in the Location Setup screen, verify that all the patches are firmly attached to the patient and properly connected. If the message does not disappear, perform the following until the patch is correctly displayed:   1. When the "Patch Detached" message appears, click Learn New. 2. Click Reset Visualization. |

If a catheter that is connected to the PIU is defective, it will not appear in the map viewers of the CARTO™ 3 System.

* For sensor-based Biosense Webster catheters, the System can detect certain defects, such as sensor failure or sensor wire failure. If one of these defects is detected, an error message or alert appears. Follow the instructions in the message to resolve the problem.
* For other defects, or for catheters without magnetic sensors, the system does not display any warning or error message addressing this issue. If this situation occurs, replace the catheter.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  In the case of an unexpected exit from a study, the System provides an option to continue the study directly, instead of continuing from Review mode. |

See also:

* ["Back Patches and Movement Identification"](#target9000048)
* ["Error Messages and Alerts"](#target9000049)
* ["Toolbars and Icons"](#target9000050)

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Setup\_Hardware\_c3\_EN.xml

### Location Setup Screen

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Setup\_Location\_c3\_v3-2\_EN.xml

The Location Setup screen is used for determining correct placement in the x-y plane (relative to the Location pad) and the z-axis (relative to the horizontal middle of the Location Pad).

![c3_v7.5_location_setup_patch_setup.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 69: Location Setup Screen

The green circles represent the back patches, which are connected to the green connectors on the Patch Unit. The yellow circles represent the chest patches, which are connected to the yellow connectors on the Patch Unit. Additional indications represent the patch status.

If magnetic sensors (either catheters or patches) are placed outside the mapping field, the CARTO™ 3 System issues an "Out of Range" message. The Location Setup screen can be used to identify which patches need repositioning. Alternatively, the Location Pad can be repositioned to bring the sensors inside the mapping field.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteCaution.png](data:image/png;base64...) | *CAUTION*  Improperly positioned patches may increase the chances of a Virtual Magnetic Reference move which is unrelated to patient movement. This could also result in inaccurate catheter visualization.  "1.11.4.1.13.2.2.6.3. 12.12.1.13.10." "1.11.4.-V11.13.2.-V12.6.3. -V112.12.1.-V113.10.-V1"  REP4611 A-V1-13.10.2, D-V1-2.6.6   * CARTO™ 3 System Patches must encircle the heart, positioned according to the instructions in the Patch IFU. * Patches must be placed on a stable part of the body to prevent their movement. For example, do not position patches over the scapula. * Do NOT place CARTO™ 3 Chest Patches over an implanted device such as an ICD. * Verify that CARTO™ 3 Chest Patches do not overlap other patches on the patient's body, including ECG and ablation patches. * Verify that patch cables do not overlap the ablation patch or the ablation patch cable and that the ablation patch cable does not cross the CARTO™ 3 Patches. Keep the ablation patch cable separate from the other cables. |

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Setup\_study\_c3\_EN.xml

**Template**

Templates are used to define layouts and initial parameters, such as the catheters which may be used for a particular type of study.

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/setup\_catheter\_c3\_v7\_EN.xml

The Catheter Setup Screen includes:

The socket icons display the connectivity status of each socket.

Right-click an empty socket for a list of defined catheters.

Devices that are recognized by the template but are not connected appear shadowed. (See [Figure 71: "Catheter Setup Screen"](#target9000051).)

* Visualization details for the selected catheter
* Mapping parameters for the selected catheter

This includes mapping channels and proximity settings.

When you connect a focal ablation catheter, the system might prompt you to select the specific type of catheter that is connected. If the wrong catheter is selected, visualization might be compromised.

#### Defining a Catheter with the Catheter Definition Tool

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/setup\_define\_catheter\_v8\_EN.xml

If a required catheter does not appear in the list of defined catheters in the Catheter Setup screen, you can use the Catheter Definition Tool to enter the catheter's parameters and add it to the list of recognized catheters.

Manufacturer values such as French size, number of electrodes, electrode spacing, and electrode width are entered in the Catheter Definition dialog box. Then a preview of the catheter image is generated for visual comparison with the actual catheter.

Characteristics such as size, shape, and electrode spacing must be entered correctly. If the values entered for the catheter are incorrect, not only does the image in the dialog box appear different from the catheter, but visualization of the catheter in the system will be distorted.

1.4.5.1 V3

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteDanger.png](data:image/png;base64...) | **WARNING**  All values must be entered exactly as provided by the catheter manufacturer. If incorrect values are entered, the danger of perforation of tissue exists due to a possible difference between the supposed values and the actual values. |

Before beginning a study, you can access the Catheter Definition Tool through the System Tools dialog box. During a study, you can access the tool from the Catheter menu.

![cathDefTool_db.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 72: Catheter Definition Dialog Box

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Open the Catheter Definition Tool:   * From the opening screen, click the System button, and then click ![System_cathDefTool_btn.png](data:image/png;base64...). * During a study, click Catheter > Catheter Definition Tool.   The Catheter Definition Tool dialog box appears.  ![Catheter_def_Tool_c3_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 2. | Click ![cathDef_btn.png](data:image/png;base64...).  The Catheter Definition dialog box appears (see [Figure 72: "Catheter Definition Dialog Box"](#target9000052)). |
| 3. | Enter the catheter name, catalog number, and manufacturer.  The Catheter Preview pane displays examples of where to find the information.  ![cathDefTool_db_manufList.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 4. | Do one of the following:   * If there is a tip electrode, select Tip electrode length and select the length from the list. * If there is not a tip electrode, clear the Tip electrode length check box and select the Distal to first electrode length (up to 20 mm). |
| 5. | Select the Spacing Method.  For Biosense Webster catheters, the measure is Center to Center. Other manufacturers use Edge to Edge. |
| 6. | Define the Spacing.   * The Spacing drop-down menu provides common options. * Choose Custom to define unique catheters. |
| 7. | Select the Electrode Width and French Size. |
| 8. | If the catheter is circular, select Circular and then select Counter Clockwise or Clockwise. |
| 9. | To verify catheter visualization, click ![btn_preview.png](data:image/png;base64...) in the Catheter Preview pane.  A preview of the catheter appears. Click and drag the image to rotate it. |
| 10. | Do one of the following:   * To save the current catheter and define an additional catheter, click Add. * To save the current catheter and exit, click OK. * To exit without saving changes, click Cancel. |

Once the catheter definition is visually validated and added to the system, the catheter appears in the defined catheters list.

| 4. | All user-defined Catheter files are exported to the selected device and the following notification is displayed: |
| 5. | To verify catheter visualization, click in the Catheter Preview pane.  ![cath_export_success_c3_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...) |

When exporting to an external storage device, user-defined catheter files are signed with a valid BWI certificate.

#### Importing User-defined Catheters

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Importing\_Userdefined\_Catheters\_v8\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Open the Biosense Webster Catheter Definition Tool |
| 2. | Click Import Catheters button. |
| 3. | Select an external storage device (USB or DVD). |
| 4. | All user-defined Catheter files are imported from the selected device and the following notification is displayed:  ![cath_import_success_c3_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...) |

When importing catheter definitions, the system checks the catheter files for integrity and a valid BWI certificate.

Table 39: Error Notifications

| Error | Notification |
| --- | --- |
| If the signature is not valid, the system cancels the operation and issues an error notification. | ![Catheter_Import_Digital_Signature_Failure_Error_c3_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| If the data integrity check fails, the system cancels the operation and issues an error notification. | ![Catheter_Import_Data_Integrity_Failure_Error_c3_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| If the signature does not exist, the system issues a warning notification, allowing you to cancel or proceed with the operation. | ![Catheter_Import _No_Signature_Error_c3_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...) |

### Map Setup Screen

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/setup\_map\_c3\_v8\_EN.xml

The Map Setup Screen is used to define the following:

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Map Type | Select Point by point (electroanatomical) or FAM  The default map type is FAM. The default location processing mode is Stable. |
| ![para_mapSetup_filterBtn_sm.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Opens the Acquisition Filters tab of the Preferences dialog box. |
| LAT Hybrid | Creates a LAT Hybrid map.[[17]](#footnote-17) |
| Add Predefined Maps | If Add Predefined Maps is selected, maps are added to the study for the left atrium, right and left superior pulmonary veins, and right and left inferior pulmonary veins. (Available only for the first map in a study.) |
| Map Name | Used to change the default map name. |
| Mapping Catheter | Used to select the mapping catheter. The list depends on the available catheters and on the acquisition mode. |
| Chamber of Interest | Indicates whether the map is: Left Atrium, Right Atrium, Left Ventricle or Right Ventricle.  You must select a chamber before you can initialize the Study and move to the mapping phase.  ![MapSetup_chamber_set_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| Reference | Select the Reference Annotation channel and the annotation criterion. Define the color of the Reference Annotation.  The Reference Channel provides a reference for the electrical signals (electrogram). In general, it is obtained from a body surface or intracardiac signal. |
| Mapping | Select the mapping criterion. Define the color of the Mapping Annotation. |
| Window of Interest | Type the values, or adjust the Window of Interest in the Annotation Viewer The default option links the LAT WOI to the Voltage WOI. Unlink them by clicking on the link icon, or using the Link LAT and Voltage WOI from the toolbox menu of Annotation Viewer, to configure each separately. |
| Fast Mapping | Determine whether FAM data will be collected during ablation, and whether EA points will automatically be combined with the reconstruction |

## Initializing Catheter Visualization

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/curve\_visualization\_initializing\_3-2\_EN.xml

Before starting initialization, verify the following:

* The ablation patch (if required) and ablation generator are connected to the system.
* The ablation generator is switched on.
* Device for real-time verification of catheter locations is ready for use.

If a fluoroscopy device is used for real-time verification, it is positioned according to guidelines.

Ablation and MAP 1-2 stimulation routing should be avoided during the initialization process. If initialization is started while stimulation to MAP 1-2 is active, the initialization process is postponed until the routing is changed. Once the stimulation routing is changed, initialization starts automatically.

If initialization is started while there is an alert message regarding metal interference, the initialization process is postponed until the alert message disappears. When the message disappears, initialization starts automatically.

14.2.4. V1

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteCaution.png](data:image/png;base64...) | *CAUTION*  Fluoroscopy devices that are too close to the patient might affect location accuracy and, as a result, catheter visualization. |

After verifying the above conditions, click ![setup_initialize_btn.png](data:image/png;base64...).

During initialization of catheter visualization:

* An alert message appears. The message will disappear when initialization ends.
* During initialization of catheter visualization, delivery of energy for ablation will be disabled for a short period. When energy for ablation is available, the impedance reading appears on the RF generator.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Do not switch the ablation generator off during initialization. |

## Delivery of RF Energy

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/rf\_delivery\_v7p3\_EN.xml

The system provides routing for the delivery of RF energy (ablation).

When RF energy is detected:

* The ablation catheter is automatically set as the mapping catheter.
* The tip of the focal ablation catheter changes from green to red.
* Points acquired during the ablation session automatically receive the Ablation tag.
* The Readings Dashboard switches to the ablation configuration.

76718

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If points are acquired manually within the first second of ablation, the point will be acquired with the previous mapping catheter. |

![ablation_indication_v6.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 76: RF Energy Indication

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If the connected ablation catheter is not compatible with the connected RF generator, an error message appears. No RF energy indication is displayed in the CARTO™ 3 System. |

When RF energy is no longer detected, the mapping catheter is automatically returned to the original setting and the Readings Dashboard returns to the mapping configuration.

RF energy delivery through the CARTO™ 3 System is not enabled in any of the following situations:

* Up to 40 seconds after clicking Initialize
* When the PIU is OFF
* Up to 40 seconds after clicking Continue Study
* Up to 40 seconds after the approval of a BCS (body coordinate system) move
* During the ~40-second recovery after a loss of communication with the PIU
* Up to 40 seconds after clicking Reset Visualization
* Up to 40 seconds after the approval of "Patch Detached" or a disconnection

The CARTO™ 3 System provides graphs displaying information from the RF generator during ablation, and a list of each completed ablation session. If communication between the CARTO™ 3 System and the generator is lost during the ablation session, a comment is added to the session data list.

### Viewing RF Session Data

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/viewing\_ablation\_rf\_data\_EN.xml

The Graphs Viewer provides a real-time display of power, temperature, and impedance over time. The display always includes data for the distal sensor. Proximal data is displayed only when using a compatible RF generator and a dual-sensor navigation and ablation catheter.[[18]](#footnote-18)

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  When the CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module is active, force data is displayed in the same viewer. The CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Dashboard might also be displayed. |

![RF_window.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 77: Graphs Viewer

After an RF session, the following data is presented in the list below the graph.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| # | The sequential number of the RF session |
| Time | The length (in seconds) of the RF session. |
| C° (avg) | The average temperature detected during the RF session. |
| W (avg) | The average power detected during the RF session. |
| Ω (avg) | The average impedance detected during the RF session. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If an incompatible RF generator is used, the CARTO™ 3 System will display only one temperature in all cases. If a dual sensor catheter is connected to the system, the temperature displayed is the highest of the temperatures read. |

### Refreshing the Graphs Viewer Display

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/RefreshGraph\_EN.xml

After an ablation session is performed or reviewed, the Graphs Viewer must be refreshed in order to resume real-time display.

To refresh the Graphs Viewer, do one of the following:

* Double click the Graphs Viewer background.
* Click the Play icon on the Dashboard.
* Acquire a new point.

The display is cleared, and a new graph begins.

### Setting the Graph Preferences

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/OpenRTGraphPref\_c3-v6\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Do one of the following:   * Click Tools > Preferences > Graphs. * Right-click the Graphs Viewer background.   Press **CTRL + ALT + P**.  The Preferences dialog box opens to the Graphs tab. There are sliders for each parameter that is displayed in the Graphs Viewer.[[19]](#footnote-19)  ![preferences_graphs_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 2. | Set each slider to the desired range.  The values on the Graphs Viewer are updated in real time.  Force settings are only available when the CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module is installed.[[20]](#footnote-20) |
| 3. | In the Time list, select the amount of time to be displayed. |
| 4. | Click Apply to save the changes and leave the dialog box open, or press OK to save the changes and close the dialog box. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  To view the RF Table (multi-channel RF generator only), click RF Table. |

## Exiting a Study

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/study\_exit\_v7p4\_EN.xml

When you exit a Study (Study > Exit) you will be prompted to record the primary arrhythmia. The secondary arrhythmia is optional.

To export study summary data from the CARTO™ 3 System to other applications, select Statistics.

It is recommended to back up all new patient studies at the end of each procedure or day. To facilitate this, you can create a backup when exiting a study (see ["When to Back Up Studies"](#target9000053)).

![Exit_study_all_blue.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 78: Exiting a Study

If an issue occurred during the study and you intend to open a complaint, select the Save Study debug files for further investigation option. This ensures that log files and other debugging information for the Study is not removed during routine Workstation maintenance. Study backup is not affected.

## Exporting Study Statistics

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/study\_statistics\_EN.xml

There are two options for exporting study statistics from the CARTO™ 3 System to other applications:

* When exiting a study, from the Exit Study dialog box, select Statistics.
* Using the menu Study > Study Statistics.

After you select one of these options, the Study Statistics window appears.

![study_statistics_export.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 79: Study Statistics Window

To export this data, click Export and then scan the QR code in the Study Statistics QR Code window.

## Review Mode

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/reviewMode\_using\_C3\_EN.xml

Review Mode enables you to review and edit data collected during a CARTO™ 3 Study. You can review a single map in a study stored on the CARTO™ 3 Workstation or a disk. Certain options that cannot be performed in Acquisition mode are accessible in Review Mode.

Review Mode is accessed from the Opening Screen.

When you select a point on the map or in the Point List, the following data is available for review:

* Point data
* Point stability
* Cycle length
* LAT
* Voltage values
* Reference and intracardiac electrograms
* Time at which a point was acquired

All changes made to the study in Review Mode are automatically saved.

![mainScreen_reviewMode.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 80: Main Screen in Review Mode

### Opening a Study in Review Mode

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/reviewMode\_Review\_study\_c3\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | On the Opening Screen, click Review Study.  The Open Study dialog box appears. By default, data is displayed by patient name (see [Figure 81: "Open Study Dialog Box with Study Selected"](#target9000054)). |
| 2. | (Optional) Sort the data: select a method for displaying data (Patient Name or Study Date), and then click a column heading to sort data.  You can sort by Last Name, First Name, ID #, and Date of Birth. When displaying data by Study Name, you can also sort by Study Name and Study Date.  When searching by Patient Name, you can enter part of the name in the Quick Search field to limit the options. |
| 3. | Select a study.  (Optional) You can also select the map that will be displayed initially. |
| 4. | Click OK. |

The study opens in Review Mode. By default, the Selected Point Viewer, Point List, Main Map Viewer, and Additional Map Viewer are available.

![openStudy_v4.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 81: Open Study Dialog Box with Study Selected

### Review Study Data

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/reviewMode\_studyData\_C3\_7-5\_EN.xml

In Review mode, you can view data from the study, including patient details and map details.

To open the Review Study Data window, click Display > Study Data.

![review_study_data_arythmia.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 82: Review Study Data Window

### Continuing a Study from Review Mode

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/reviewMode\_continue\_study\_c3\_EN.xml

After opening a study in Review Mode, you can continue mapping.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |

| 2. | Select Study > Continue Study. |

The System changes to Acquisition Mode.

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/LoadingMap\_prevStudy\_v6\_EN.xml

## Exiting the CARTO™ 3 System

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/system\_exit\_c3\_v6p5\_EN.xml

| 2. | Select the desired option and click OK.   |  |  | | --- | --- | | **Option** | **Result** | | Reload CARTO™ Application | The application restarts, without restarting the workstation | | Reboot | The workstation restarts. | | Shut Down | The automatic download of new antivirus definition files is performed, when configured.  The workstation shuts down. | | Scan & Shut Down | When the antivirus application is enabled, an additional option appears: Scan & Shut Down.  The automatic download of new antivirus definition files is performed, when configured.  When selected, a scan for viruses is performed before the workstation shuts down. | |

## Workstation Maintenance and Optimization

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/performance\_optimization\_v8p2\_EN.xml

For consistent response time, the CARTO™ 3 System will regularly perform maintenance and optimization tasks at time of shutdown. These tasks include: defragmentation on the workstation hard disk and updating of antivirus definition files.

If the defragmentation process is scheduled to occur, it starts the next time the workstation is shut down.

The optimization time depends on the number of studies. If the process will take more than a few minutes, the option to cancel appears. Click Cancel to stop the process.

![shut_down_maint.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 85: Hard Disk Optimization Dialog Box

If you cancel the maintenance and optimization process, and the disk fragmentation is more than 30% complete, a message appears with the option to continue the process. You must click Yes to cancel the maintenance and continue shutting down the workstation. Click No to continue with the process.

![shut_down_confirm_cancel.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 86: Hard Disk Optimization Confirmation

# CARTO™ 3 Interface

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/intro\_to\_interface\_c3\_EN.xml

## Overview of the Main Screen

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/main\_screen\_overview\_v7\_EN.xml

The main screen appears after you complete the Setup phase or when you open a study for review. All mapping and related activities are performed from here.

In Acquisition mode, the Mapping, Ablation, and Verification views present different layouts optimized for each phase of the study. You can add or remove viewers and make other changes to the appearance of each layout. These changes can be saved to a custom template.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Mapping View | Main Map Viewer, Additional Map Viewer, Monitor Pane  ![fullScreen_mapping_c3-v7.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| Ablation View | Selected Point Viewer, Main Map Viewer, Additional Map Viewer, Annotation Viewer, Graphs Viewer, Monitor Pane  ![fullScreen_ablation_c3-v7.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| Verification View | Selected Point Viewer, Point List, Main Map Viewer, Annotation Viewer, Monitor Pane  ![fullScreen_verification_c3-v7.png](data:image/png;base64...) |

The Main Map Viewer is always open, in both Acquisition Mode and Review Mode. The remaining viewers can be opened or closed as desired.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  To change the font size in the Point List, ECG viewers, Color Scale bar, or Graphs Viewer, press the ALT key while rotating the mouse wheel. |

The table below describes the key elements of the main screen. The available options vary, depending on the installed software modules.

Table 40: Key to Elements of the Main Screen

| Element | Function |
| --- | --- |
| Menu Bar | Provides access to the main system functions  ![main_menus_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| Main Toolbar | Provides quick access buttons for common tasks  ![main_toolbar_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| Status Bar | Displays connectivity status and error handling icons  ![main_status_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| Setup | Provides access to the Setup Screens.  ![setupButtons_v6.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| Main Screen Views | Switches between the main screen views.  To rename a view, click the text. When the text is highlighted, enter the new name.  ![main_phases_4-3.png](data:image/png;base64...) |

See also:

* ["ECG Viewers"](#target9000056)
* ["Menus, Shortcuts, and Related Icons"](#target9000057)
* ["Setup Guides"](#target9000058)

### Floating Window Placement

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/mainScreen\_WindowPlacement\_v6.xml

Most preferences and other settings are entered through a floating window or dialog box.

The first time the floating window is opened, it appears at the upper right of the screen. If you move the window to a new position, the System remembers the position. The next time the window is opened, it appears in the same position.

You can override this behavior by clicking the Pin icon in the upper right corner of the floating window.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Some floating window images in this document might not display the Pin icon. |

### Additional Map Viewer

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/additional\_view\_c3\_v6\_EN.xml

The Additional Map Viewer is a second map viewer that enables you to display a map from two different angles, or to view different maps within the same study.

![additional_view_c3-v6.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 87: Additional Map Viewer

The Synchronization drop-down menu enables you to control the map orientation and display options in the Additional Map Viewer.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| None | Independent from Main Map Viewer |
| Sync | Same orientation and display options as the Main Map Viewer. This includes background display options |
| Comp | Complementary to the Main Map Viewer, as shown in the following table. |

Table 43: Complementary views displayed in the Additional Map Viewer

| **Main Map Viewer** | **Additional Map Viewer** | **Main Map Viewer** | **Additional Map Viewer** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AP | RL | LAO | RAO |
| PA | LL | RAO | LAO |
| Superior (SUP) | AP | LL | PA |
| Inferior (INF) | AP | RL | AP |

The map viewer is updated.

### Glass View

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/c3\_interface\_glassView\_7\_EN.xml

**Glass View** is a coloring option with a glass-like presentation of the map shell. Glass View is activated in the Map Transparency dialog box. Glass View applies only to the Active Map. Glass View does not affect the visualization settings (point marks, projections, tags, etc.).

![map_glassView_transparency_7.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 88: Glass View

### Glass Mode

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/c3\_interface\_glassMode\_EN.xml

**Glass Mode** reduces the amount of data presented in the map viewers. Glass Mode uses Glass View coloring. Map transparency is set to 60%, and all points except the selected point are hidden.

To turn Glass Mode on and off, click the Glass Mode toggle button. The Glass Mode buttons in the two viewers are independent of each other.

![map_glassView_button.png](data:image/png;base64...)

![map_glassView_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 89: Glass Mode (left) and Normal View (right)

In Glass Mode, tags of points with inconsistent or no LAT values are hidden. Display of other tags depends on the Visualization Setup settings.

The system automatically exits Glass Mode in the following situations:

* Creating a new map or remap
* Saving a map with a different name
* During vessel acquisition
* Loading CT or MRI images
* Loading a map from a previous study
* Creating an ultrasound contour

### Viewing the Point Tool Tip

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/c3\_mapViewer\_pointTooltips\_v6\_EN.xml

The **Point Tool Tip** presents data for the selected point in a graphical tool tip in the Main Map Viewer. The data includes the Body Surface II channel, the reference channel (if different), the mapping channel, and other acquisition data for the selected point.

In the Point Tool Tip, you can rearrange signals, change the gain, and edit the mapping annotation.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | To view the tool tip, press the **E** key and move the cursor over the point.  The point is selected. The tool tip appears in the Main Map Viewer.  ![pointToolTip.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 2. | To resize the Point Tool Tip, drag the lower right corner. |
| 3. | To close the Point Tool Tip, click the Main Map Viewer background, or click the ![x_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...) in the upper right corner. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  The point tool tip is only available for points in the Active Map. |

### Changing Map Orientation and Projection

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/c3\_map\_changingOrientation\_c3\_v4\_EN.xml

There are several methods for changing the orientation and projection of a map.

Table 44: Methods for Changing Map Orientation

| Method | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Rotate map with mouse | Click the scroll wheel and drag the map in the desired direction, and release the wheel when you have reached the desired rotation.   |  |  | | --- | --- | | ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Avoid clicking toolbars when rotating the map. | |
| Rotate map with keyboard | Click the map first, then use the keyboard to rotate the map as follows:   * x axis: Up and Down arrows * y axis: Left and Right arrows * x axis: Home and End |
| Select a standard projection | Click a standard projection (AP, PA, etc.) from the list under the Projection Icon.  ![gui_orientation_icon_v6.png](data:image/png;base64...)  LAO and RAO views are applied according to the current settings (Map > LAO/RAO Orientation). |
| Turn map to point | To rotate the map and center to a point, right-click the point in either the Selected Point Viewer or the Point List and select Turn Map to Point.  ![TurnMapToPoint_SPV.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...) |

#### Defining the LAO and RAO Orientation

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/c3\_mapViewer\_orientation\_setLAO-RAO\_c3\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Click Map > LAO/RAO Orientation. |
| 2. | Select the orientation you want.  The options are RAO -45, LAO -45 (default) and RAO -30, LAO -60. |

#### Defining a Custom Projection

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/c3\_mapViewer\_orientation\_custom\_v6\_EN.xml

You can define up to four custom projections.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Rotate the map to the desired view. |
| 2. | Right-click the Projection icon and select Define.  ![mapViewer_projectionIcon_defineNew_v6.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 3. | Click the desired view number.  The projection is saved. |
| 4. | To return to the custom view, right-click the Projection icon and select the desired view number. |

The user-defined projections can be saved to a template.

### Manually Zooming In or Out of a Map

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Zooming In and Out of a Map\_EN.xml

The Zoom wheel is used to increase or decrease the display size of the map. The distance scale is indicated by the value next to the zoom icon and represents the number of cm per square of the grid, relative to the zoomed size of the image. The default display is 1 cm per square of the grid.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  To automatically zoom in when switching between maps, click Display > Enable Auto Zoom. |

#### Increasing or Decreasing the Zoom of the Map

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/c3\_mapViewer\_zoom\_change\_EN.xml

You can zoom in or out of a map using the mouse or the Zoom wheel in the map viewers.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Do one of the following:   * Click and drag the Zoom wheel (bottom right of the map viewer) up or down to zoom in or out of the display, respectively. * Click the scroll button anywhere in the map viewer, and scroll up or down to the required zoom.   The distance scale changes as the zoom increases or decreases. |
| 2. | To return to the default zoom, click the gray ball under the zoom wheel. |

### Panning In on an Area of Interest

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/c3\_mapViewer\_pan\_c3\_EN.xml

You can move the maps or images in the display, to enable a better view of an area of interest.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |

| 2. | To automatically center all maps and images, click ![c3_icon_panON.png](data:image/png;base64...).  The map viewer returns to a display with all maps and images centered in the desired window.  The Auto Center icon changes to reflect that all maps and images in the display are automatically centered (![c3_icon_panOFF.png](data:image/png;base64...)). |

### Displaying and Working with Multiple Maps

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/c3\_maps\_displayingMultiple\_c3\_v7\_EN.xml

Up to 35 maps can be created during a study. This can include contours and images (depending on the available modules). These maps and images are listed in the Map List.

All maps can be displayed. However, location data is always associated with one *active map*. The name of the active map is displayed at the top of the Map List, with the number of points and contours. A tool tip provides additional data.

You can use the Map List to hide or show maps and images, and to change the active map. If the Map List is hidden, click the arrow next to the Active Map name.

![MapList_open_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 90: Map List

#### Changing the Active Map

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/c3\_mapViewer\_change\_activeMap\_c3\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | If the Map List is closed, click the arrow next to the Active Map's name.  The list of maps in the current study opens. |
| 2. | Click the circle next to the map you want to have as the Active Map. |

An outline is drawn around the active map in the Main Map Viewer. Any new points that are acquired will be added to the active map.

18289

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If a gated map is selected as the Active Map, the focal ablation catheter is automatically set as the mapping catheter. |

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/c3\_mapViewer\_hide\_show\_maps\_c3\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |

| 2. | Select the check box next to each map you want to show. Clear the check box next to each map you want to hide. |

With each selection, the map viewer is updated.

### Using the Clipping Plane

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/c3\_maps\_clippingPlane\_EN.xml

To receive a clearer image of a specific area, the displayed maps can be cut along a plane at a distance from the center of the reconstructed map.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Right-click the background of a map viewer and select Clipping Plane.  The clipping plane appears. |
| 2. | Click and drag the corners of the frame to move the clipping plane along the x-y axis.  The cursor icon is updated.  ![clippingPlane_move_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 3. | Click and drag any side of the frame to rotate the clipping plane freely in any direction.  The cursor icon is updated.  ![clippingPlane_rotate_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 4. | To change the orientation of the clipping plane along a different axis or in a different direction, right-click the frame and select one of the following options:   |  |  | | --- | --- | | Flip | The clipping plane flips to the opposite direction. | | Coronal | The clipping plane moves to the coronal projection. | | Sagittal | The clipping plane moves to the sagittal projection. | | Transversal | The clipping plane moves to the transversal projection. | | Reset | The clipping plane is reset parallel to the display in the map viewer. | | Exit | Closes the clipping plane. | |

![clippingPlane.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 91: Clipping Plane

## Point List

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/point\_list\_c3\_v7\_EN.xml

When you acquire a point, it immediately appears in the Point List. The Point List provides access to point data for the Active Map.

In the Point List, you can:

* Scroll through the list and view the values for all points (the displayed point values vary, depending on the type of map displayed, such as LAT or bipolar voltage).
* Apply tags and comments to points.
* Delete and restore points.

![pointList_recycleBin_c3-v4.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 92: Point List and Recycle Bin

If Continuous Mapping is active and the Point List is sorted by anything other than index number, the sorting is not updated until Continuous Mapping stops. An icon appears by the column to indicate that sorting is not up to date. The list is sorted automatically when Continuous Mapping stops.

![pointList_notSorted_CL.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 93: Point List During Continuous Mapping, Sorted by Cycle Length

When you select a point in the Point List, the corresponding point in the Map is selected. Selecting a point in the map selects it in the Point List.

![pointList_MapViewer_selected_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 94: Point Selected in Point List and Main Map Viewer

### Point List Data

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/point\_list\_using\_c3\_v6\_EN.xml

The following table describes standard columns in the Point List. Other columns may be added depending on the active modules and features.

Table 45: Standard Columns in the Point List

| Data | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Number | Displays the sequential number assigned to each point. |
| Type | Displays the data type (point or contour) |
| Respiration | Indicates whether the Respiration Gated filter was active when points were acquired. |
| LAT | Displays the LAT value. |
| Imp | Displays the impedance value. |
| Bi | Displays the bipolar value (mV). |
| Uni | Displays the unipolar value (mV). |
| Elect. | Displays the electrode or electrode pair associated with the point. |
| Cycle Length | Displays the cycle length of the point. |
| Tag | Displays the label for any tag(s) applied to the point. Select Point > Tag & Type to edit the tag. |
| Type (Point) | Displays the type assigned to the point. Select Point > Tag & Type to edit the type. |
| Interfaced | Points that are acquired while the CARTO™ 3 System is interfaced with an EP Recording System are marked with a check mark. |
| Time | Displays the time at which the point was acquired. |
| Comment | Displays any comments assigned to the point. To add comments, double-click in the comments field. |
| AppliedToMap | Indicates that an EA point is applied to a FAM reconstruction. |

You can sort the Point List data by clicking the column headers.

You can add or remove columns. Right-click a column to view the options, and then select or clear the column names. The available options may vary, depending on the installed software modules.

![pointList_options_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 95: Available Columns in the Point List

### Adding or Editing a Comment

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ptList\_add\_edit\_comment\_c3\_EN.xml

You can add or edit comments for all points displayed in the Point List.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Click in the comment field of a selected point. |
| 2. | Type the comment and press Enter. |

The comment is added to the field in the Point List.

## ECG Viewers

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ECG\_Viewers\_c3\_EN.xml

The CARTO™ 3 System presents ECG signals and timing indications (for example, annotations) in the following ways:

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Monitor | Displays the signals in real time. |
| Annotation Viewer | Displays the signals in real time, with every annotation aligned in the same relative position in the window.  An overlay of the previous beat can be displayed. |
| Selected Point Viewer | Displays the currently-selected point. |
| Baseline Point Viewer | Displays the point selected to be the baseline. This viewer is not refreshed unless a new baseline point is selected.  For example, a reference point can be acquired in a specific location before the application of ablation energy. This point is displayed in the Baseline Point Viewer. Another point is acquired in the same location after the application of ablation energy, and viewed in the Selected Point Viewer. Real-time signals from the same location can be viewed in the Annotation Viewer. |

You can align the ECG viewers to compare signals. A predefined layout opens all the ECG viewers, the Main Map Viewer, and the Graphs Viewer. The viewers are synchronized in the display of selected ECG channels, gain, and order.

![predefined_layouts_ECG.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 96: Synchronized ECG Viewer Layout

![nmarq_ECGviewers_syn_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 97: ECG Viewers

22438, VSTS

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  When the viewers are in Synchronized Mode, the upper signal in the Baseline Point Viewer moves when a new point is set as the baseline point. |

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ecg\_viewer\_overlay\_EN.xml

You can display an overlay of the previous beat in the Annotation Viewer.

|  |  |

| 2. | Select Show Previous Beat.  ![annotation_viewer_settings.png](data:image/png;base64...) |

### Selected Point Viewer

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/selected\_point\_viewer\_c3\_v4\_EN.xml

The Selected Point Viewer gives a readout of the conditions of a specific point.

The Stability Bars are displayed in the Selected Point Viewer, together with the CL, LAT, unipolar (Uni) voltage, and bipolar (Bi) voltage for each beat.

The Stability Bars and point values can be used to evaluate the point. If necessary, the point can be discarded (via the Point List or map) or edited (via the Selected Point Viewer).

The Beat Buffer stores the last 10 beats for the last acquired point. You can select the most suitable heartbeat to be the acquired point.

Clicking a mapping channel in the Selected Point Viewer selects the point in the Point List and map viewers.

![ECG_selectedPointViewer_v3-2.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 101: Selected Point Viewer

#### Stability Bars

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/selectedPointViewer\_stabilityBars\_c3\_EN.xml

The stability bars provide an indication of catheter and channel stability, based on tip point data. If the stability bars are more gray than green, stability is marginal (see [Figure 102: "Stability Bars"](#target9000060)).

In hard-to-reach locations, a marginal reading may be the best you can obtain. The decision on whether or not to delete the point is up to you.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Stability Bar | Information Displayed |
| CL | The percent difference between the current cycle length and the median cycle length of all points in the current map. The entire bar represents a 20% difference. |
| LAT | A measure of how stable the local activation time is between beats. Gray represents the measured difference between the LATs of consecutive beats, in ms. The entire bar represents 10 ms. |
| Loc | A measure of stability between the current and previous beat. Gray indicates the measured location difference between beats. The entire bar represents a difference of 12 mm. |

![stabilityBars_v2.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 102: Stability Bars

#### Beat Buffer

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ecg\_beatBuffer\_6p5\_EN.xml

When a point or point group is acquired, the system temporarily saves the last 10 beats and stores them in the Beat Buffer. The point data of all 10 beats can be viewed and edited. You can select the most suitable heartbeat to be the acquired point.

The Beat Buffer is expanded by default. Click the double arrows (![arrows_collapse.png](data:image/png;base64...)) to collapse or expand the beat buffer.

![beatBuffer_expanded_6p5.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 103: Beat Buffer: Expanded

![beatBuffer_collapsed_6p5.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 104: Beat Buffer: Collapsed

To select a new beat, click the number or use the arrows to scroll through the beats in order. The Selected Point Viewer and the map are updated according to the new values.

The changes are saved as soon as you acquire a new point or select a different point.

### Baseline Point Viewer

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/baseline\_point\_viewer\_c3-2\_EN.xml

The Baseline Point Viewer is used to continuously display one user-selected point. This point is used as a reference point, while other data is displayed in the Selected Point Viewer and the Annotation Viewer.

The Baseline Point Viewer has the same features as the Selected Point Viewer.

#### Adding a Point to the Baseline Point Viewer

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ecg\_viewer\_addBasePoint\_c3-2\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Right-click the point. |
| 2. | Select Show as Baseline. |

The point is displayed in the Baseline Point Viewer.

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ecg\_viewer\_customization\_v6\_EN.xml

|  |  |

### Measuring Time in ECG Viewers

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ecg\_viewer\_measureAnnotationTime\_v6\_EN.xml

You can use the Time Caliper to measure elapsed time in the ECG viewers.

### Measuring Voltage in the ECG Viewers

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ecg\_viewer\_measureVoltage\_v6\_EN.xml

You can use the Voltage Caliper to measure voltage in one of the ECG viewers.

### Working with Channels

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/working\_with\_channels\_v7\_EN.xml

By default, all the ECG Viewers display the Reference electrogram (EGM) and Mapping EGM channels. In addition, you can customize each viewer in the following ways:

* Hide or show additional channels.
* Change the order of channels.
* Change the time scale for all channels.
* Change the gain for a signal or for all channels.

Changes can be saved to a new template.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Only recorded channels are available. |

#### Adding and Removing Channels

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ecg\_viewer\_addRemoveChannels\_7p5\_EN.xml

|  |  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1. | Click ![ecgv_add_channels_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...).  A list of available catheters appears. If a catheter is not connected to the PIU, it is marked (N/C).  ![monitorChannelOptions_catList.png](data:image/png;base64...)   |  |  | | --- | --- | | ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  The Selected Point Viewer also has an option to hide channels from all other catheters. | |
| 2. | Select a catheter.  A list of channels appears. Mapping channels are selected by default. Other channels can be selected or cleared as needed.  ![monitorChannelOptions_map_c3-v7.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 3. | Select the appropriate option.  Changes are immediately applied. |
| 4. | To change the order for a particular catheter, right-click a signal and select Reorder.  All catheters can be presented in ascending or descending order. Branched catheters can also be presented in rings.  ![ECG_branched_reordered_ring.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 5. | To hide active channels in the Annotation Viewer, right-click an active channel and then click Hide.  The active channel is hidden from the display.  To restore the hidden channels, click the refresh button ![refreshChannel.png](data:image/png;base64...).  ![hideActiveMappingChannels.png](data:image/png;base64...) |

#### Changing the Time Scale for all Channels in the Viewer

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ecg\_viewer\_changeTmeScale\_c3\_EN.xml

You can set the time scale in any ECG Viewer to 400, 200, 100, or 50 mm/sec. The Selected Point Viewer can also be set to 25 or 12.5 mm/sec.

|  |  |

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ecg\_viewer\_changeGain\_c3\_EN.xml

#### ECG Presets

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ECG\_Presets\_v7-5\_EN.xml

There are three default preset tabs in the Monitorand Annotation Viewer.

![preset_tabs.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| **ECG Preset Tabs** |  |
| The selected preset tab is shown with orange bar above it. | |
| Each tab can contain:   * One unique preset from the full list of presets. * A menu listing all other presets as well as the secondary menu for available operations for this preset (Save, Save As, Rename, Restore, Delete). | ![ecg_tab_menu.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...) |
| * All presets can be modified. Three presets are provided as the default and a maximum of fifteen presets are possible for each viewer. | ![preset_tabs2.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...) |

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| ![ECG_Preset_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Opens the ECG Presets menu. |
| Study Default | Applies the default preset.   |  |  | | --- | --- | | ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Changes made to the default preset are saved with the study. If you continue the study from Review Mode, the saved settings are applied. | |
| [User Preset 1] | If there are user-defined presets, click to apply. |
| ![ECG_Preset_tools_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Opens a sub-menu with options for deleting or renaming user-defined presets. |
| Save | Saves any changes to the active preset. |
| Save As | Creates a new preset with the current configuration. |

# Map and Study Tools

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Map\_Study\_Tools\_xp\_EN.xml

The CARTO™ 3 System provides tools for displaying map and study data. These tools help to easily identify information data of particular interest to you. In addition, you can print maps to paper or graphic file formats, and use special tools to enhance the display of specific sites on a map. You can also create templates that save information for specific types of studies (such as the settings in the Map and Catheter Setup screens), customize the appearance of the main screen, and export maps and related data for use elsewhere.

## Point Tags

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Point\_tags\_EN.xml

Point tags are used to mark a specific point in the map for easy reference. The CARTO™ 3 Software provides several predefined point tags, but you can also create your own. Once a point tag is applied, you can change how the tagged point contributes data to the reconstruction by changing the tag type.

Five keyboard shortcuts are available for the point tags you use most often (CTRL + 1 through CTRL + 5).

Points acquired during ablation automatically receive an ablation tag.

1.8.1. V1

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteDanger.png](data:image/png;base64...) | **WARNING**  To avoid causing AV node block and serious injury to the patient, confirm that anatomical tagging is correct. |

### Tag Type

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/pointTag\_understandingType\_EN.xml

The tag type determines how the tagged point's data contributes to a reconstruction, as follows:

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Floating | The point data is acquired, but it does not contribute to the shape or color of the map. The tagged point therefore appears to be floating. |
| Location Only | The point contributes only anatomical information to the map. The electrical information is saved with the point but is not applied to the map. This is the default tag type. |
| Scar | The tagged point is marked as a scar, and, depending on the scar settings, is displayed with only a tag, or with dark gray coloring applied to the map. The point does not contribute electrical data to the map. |
| Normal | The measured electrical data of the tagged point is applied to the map. The point is part of the anatomical reconstruction. |

### Applying Point Tags

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/pointTag\_apply\_c3\_EN.xml

Point tags can be applied to points via the Point List or directly via the map.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Select the point.  Use the CTRL key to select more than one point. |
| 2. | Do one of the following:   * Click Point > Point Tag & Type. * Right-click the point on the map or in the Point List, and select Tag & Type. * Press the F8 function key.   The Point Tag dialog box appears (see [Figure 105: "Point Tag Dialog Box"](#target9000062)). |
| 3. | Select the required tag.  The change is applied in the Point List and the map viewers. |
| 4. | (Optional) Click another point and apply a tag. |
| 5. | Click OK when you are finished.  The Point Tag dialog box closes. |

![Point_tag_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 105: Point Tag Dialog Box

### Changing Tag and Type After Applying Tags

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/pointTag\_change\_tagAndType\_c3\_v4\_EN.xml

You can change the type of individual tags, as they are applied, or globally using the Point Tag Setup dialog box. You can also quickly change the tag type using the Point shortcut menu (on the map).

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Select the point whose tag type you want to change. |
| 2. | Select a point and do one of the following:   * Click Point > Point Tag & Type. * Right-click the point on the map or in the Point List, and select Tag & Type. * Press the F8 function key.   The Point Tag dialog box appears. |
| 3. | To change the tag, click a different Point Tag.  The change appears immediately in the Main Map Viewer and the Point List. The Point Tag dialog box remains open. |
| 4. | To change the type, select the type from the Selected Point Type list. |
| 5. | Click OK when you are finished.  The Point Tag dialog box closes. |

### Creating a New Point Tag

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/pointTag\_customize\_new\_c3-v4\_EN.xml

You can create new point tags via the Tag Setup dialog box, and give them the properties (color, type, label) that you want.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Open the Tag Setup dialog box. |
| 2. | Click ![addUserPointTag_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...).  A new row appears in the Tag Setup dialog box. |
| 3. | Right-click the color icon and select a color.  The selected color will be applied to the tag on the map. |
| 4. | In the Tag Name field, enter the name of the tag. |
| 5. | In the Label field, enter up to four letters that uniquely identify the tag.  These letters will appear in the Tag field of the Point List, and as a label next to the tagged point in the map. |
| 6. | In the Type field, select the type to apply to the point. |
| 7. | To assign a keyboard shortcut, select the shortcut from the list. |
| 8. | Click Apply.  The new tag type is added to the list. The keyboard shortcut is assigned to the new tag, and removed from the tag it was previously assigned to. |
| 9. | To delete a user-defined tag from the list, select the tag and click ![btn_delete_trash.png](data:image/png;base64...). |
| 10. | Click ![pointTag_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...) to return to the Point Tag dialog box and begin using the new tag. |

### Customizing Predefined Point Tags

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/pointTag\_customize\_predefined\_c3-v4\_EN.xml

The system provides several predefined tags. Although you cannot change their name or color, you can change their Type and Label.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | With a point selected, click Point > Point Tag & Type.  The Point Tag dialog box appears. |
| 2. | Click ![tagSetup_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...).  The Tag Setup dialog box appears.  ![tag_setup_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 3. | To edit Point Tags, select Point Tag Setup. To edit Contour Tags (CARTOSOUND™ Module), select Contour Tag Setup.  The appropriate tags are displayed. |
| 4. | Click the tag to be edited.  Customizable fields become editable. |
| 5. | In the Label field, type the new label (up to four characters). |
| 6. | In the Type drop-down menu, select the type to apply to the tag. |
| 7. | In the Size drop-down menu, select the size to apply to the tag. |
| 8. | To assign a keyboard shortcut, select the shortcut from the list.  When the changes are applied, the keyboard shortcut is assigned to the new tag, and removed from the tag it was previously assigned to. |
| 9. | Click Apply. |

Your changes apply to tags applied from this point forward. They do not apply to previously applied tags.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If you want to change the type of tags that were applied previously, use the Point Tag dialog box (see ["Changing Tag and Type After Applying Tags"](#target9000063)). |

### Customizing the Point Tag Appearance

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/pointTag\_customize\_display\_c3\_EN.xml

You can customize the way all point tags appear in the Main Map Viewer by changing their size and by showing or hiding the point tag symbol.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | With a point selected, click Point > Point Tag & Type.  The Point Tag dialog box appears. |
| 2. | Click the Tag Setup button (![tagSetup_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...)).  The Tag Setup dialog appears. |
| 3. | To change the Point Tag size, drag the Point Size slider to the required value, where 1 is the smallest and 4 is the largest.  Changes are applied immediately. |
| 4. | To display Point Tag labels, check the Display tag label checkbox. To hide the labels, clear the check box. |
| 5. | Click OK to close the dialog box. |

### Configuring Point Mark and Point Tag Display

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/pointTag\_visualization\_v7\_EN.xml

You can control the way point marks and point tags are displayed for each map in the Main Map Viewer or Additional Map Viewer. Ablation point tags and non-ablation point tags are configured separately.[[21]](#footnote-21)

These settings are controlled through the Visualization Setup dialog box, which is accessed through the map's right-click menu.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  For selected points, both the point mark and the projection are displayed, regardless of the visualization settings. |

![visualization_setup_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 106: Visualization Setup Dialog Box

Table 46: Visualization Setup Options

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Non-Ablation Point Tags | * On Points: Show/hide point tag (sphere) * Projected: Show/hide projected tag (disk) in FAM maps |
| Ablation Point Tags | * On Points: Show/hide ablation tag (sphere) * Projected: Show/hide projected ablation tag (disk) in FAM maps |
| Highlight Points | Late Annotation: Static Highlight/un-highlight late annotated points on map |
| Point Marks | * On Points: Show/hide point marker (white cross) * Projected: Show/hide projected tag (colored circle) in FAM maps * Manually Acquired Only: When selected, automatically acquired points are hidden * Filtered: Show/hide markers for points marked by the Internal Points filter (red cross). Available when internal point filtering is active and On Points is selected * Mark Size: Adjust size of projected marks |
| CARTOFINDER™ | * Site Indications: Show/hide CARTOFINDER™ Module sites * All ROI electrodes: When selected, all ROI electrodes are displayed, even for CARTOFINDER™ sites that are not selected. When cleared, ROI electrodes are only displayed for CARTOFINDER™ sites that are selected. |
| Apply to All Maps | Applies the current settings to every map in both viewers.  If you make additional changes after clicking Apply to All Maps, the changes apply only to the current map in the current map viewer. Click the button again to propagate the changes. |

## Anatomical Structures

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Anatomical\_tags\_EN.xml

Anatomical structures are used to mark a specific anatomical location on the map for reference. You can choose which of these structures to display, change their color, and create new ones, as needed.

### Identifying an Anatomical Structure

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/anatomicalStructure\_identify\_c3\_7p3\_EN.xml

You can mark anatomical structures such as valves, vessels, the OS, or other circular anatomical locations. The color of an anatomical structure can be changed or it can be represented as a ring on the map.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Anatomical structures are displayed in both map viewers, but they can only be created or edited in the Main Map Viewer. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Select Map > Anatomical Structure.  You can also right-click the map and select Anatomical Structure.  The Anatomical Structure dialog box appears.  ![anatomicalStructure_db_v4.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 2. | In the Main Map Viewer, do one of the following:   * Create an Anatomical structure in one click by holding the CTRL key while clicking on the relevant location of the anatomical structure. * Click three or more points on the map that best define the borders of the anatomical structure.   Each time you click, a marker is added. When there are three markers, they are joined in a dashed ring. Continue adding markers or drag the ring to get the required shape.  ![anatomicalStructure_1_xmpl.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 3. | (Optional) To delete one or more markers, right-click the marker and do one of the following:   * Click Delete Marker to delete only the selected marker. * Click Delete All Markers to delete all markers.   The marker or markers are deleted. The Anatomical Structure dialog box remains open, and you can continue marking points. |
| 4. | In the Anatomical Structure dialog box, select the name of the structure.  **SVC, IVC, CS-OS, Tricuspid Annulus, Mitral Annulus, Aortic Annulus, Pulmonary Annulus, RS Pulmonary Vein, RI Pulmonary Vein, LS Pulmonary Vein, LI Pulmonary Vein, LAA.**  The color of the ring changes according to the predefined color for each structure. |
| 5. | (Optional) Click Display inverse area to select everything outside the ring. |
| 6. | Do one of the following:   * Click Apply to save the current structure and add a new one. * Click OK to save the current structure and close the Anatomical Structure dialog box.   The outline becomes a solid line. The area inside the ring is removed. |

You can edit the anatomical structure. Click Map > Anatomical Structure if the Anatomical Structure dialog box is not open. Click the ring. It becomes a dashed line. Continue adding markers or dragging the outline as described above.

### Hiding an Anatomical Structure

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/anatomicalStructure\_hide\_c3\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Right-click the anatomical structure. |
| 2. | Click Hide. |

The anatomical structure is hidden.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  You can also hide an anatomical structure by clicking Display > Anatomical Structure and clearing the check box next to the name of the structure you want to hide. |

### Displaying an Anatomical Structure

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/anatomicalStructure\_display\_c3\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Click Display > Anatomical Structure and check the check box next to the name of the structure you want to display.  The anatomical structure is displayed. |
| 2. | Repeat for each anatomical structure you want to display. |

### Deleting an Anatomical Structure

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/anatomicalTag\_delete\_EN.xml

Anatomical Structures can be permanently deleted from a map.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Right-click the Anatomical Structure. |
| 2. | Select Delete. |

The Anatomical Structure is deleted.

## Creating Video Clips and Screen Captures

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/creating\_video\_and\_image\_captures\_c3\_EN.xml

You can create screen captures or video clips of all or part of the main screen.

The Capture Preference dialog box is used to specify the region of the screen you want to include.

The Capture button on the toolbar starts the capture. The button can be set to either images or video.

After you create the required video or image files, they can be copied to a disk for your use via the Export Data tool.

### Setting the Screen and Video Capture Parameters

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/set\_capture\_area\_EN.xml

The Screen and Video Capture Parameters define the area of the screen that will be included when capturing an image or video clip. Image and video preferences are set in the same dialog box, but they can have different settings.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Click Tools > Screen Capture Parameters.  The Capture Preference dialog box opens.  ![capturePreference_db.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 2. | Select the area to capture for both Snap (screen captures) and Video.   |  |  | | --- | --- | | Region | Select a region of the main screen at the time of the image or video capture. | | Window | Capture everything in the specified pane. Main Map Viewer is selected by default. | | Full | Capture the full screen. |   You can select different options for image and video. |
| 3. | Click OK.  The changes are saved. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  You can also access the Capture Preference dialog box from the Capture button drop-down list.  ![capture_options_c3-v4.png](data:image/png;base64...) |

### Setting the Video or Screen Capture Type

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/set\_capture\_type\_EN.xml

Before you can create screen captures or video clips, you must set the capture type.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Click the Capture button drop-down list.  The Capture shortcut menu appears. |
| 2. | Click the required option.  The Capture button is updated to show the current capture type. If the area to capture is set to Full, an indicator appears on the button, as shown in the following table. |

Table 47: Capture Button Status

| Icon | Status |
| --- | --- |
| ![captureImage_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Image Capture |
| ![captureVideo_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Video Capture |
| ![captureImage_icon_fullScreen.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Image Capture for full screen |
| ![captureVideo_icon_fullScreen.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Video Capture for full screen |

### Creating a Video Clip of the Full Screen or Window

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/create\_video\_capture\_full\_EN.xml

Follow these steps when the area to capture is set to Full or Window (see ["Setting the Screen and Video Capture Parameters"](#target9000064)) and the capture type is set to Video Capture (see ["Setting the Video or Screen Capture Type"](#target9000065)).

| --- | --- |
| 1. | Click ![captureVideo_icon_fullScreen.png](data:image/png;base64...).  The border of the area to capture flashes, and the Capture button changes to the End Capture button (![captureVideo_end_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...)). |

| 3. | Click ![captureVideo_end_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...).  The Capture File Name dialog box appears. |

| 3. | Click ![capture_check.png](data:image/png;base64...).  The border of the area to capture flashes, and the Capture button changes to the End Capture button (![captureVideo_end_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...)). |
| 4. | Perform the actions you want to record. |
| 5. | Click ![captureVideo_end_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...).  The Capture File Name dialog box appears. |
| 6. | Type the file name and click OK. |

The screen capture is saved to the Workstation hard drive.

### Creating a Screen Capture of the Full Screen or Window

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/create\_image\_capture\_full\_EN.xml

Follow these steps when the area to capture is set to Full or Window (see ["Setting the Screen and Video Capture Parameters"](#target9000068)) and the capture type is set to Image Capture (see ["Setting the Video or Screen Capture Type"](#target9000069)).

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Click ![captureImage_icon_fullScreen.png](data:image/png;base64...).  The Capture File Name dialog box appears. |

### Creating a Screen Capture of a Region of the Screen

Follow these steps when the area to capture is set to Region (see ["Setting the Screen and Video Capture Parameters"](#target9000070)) and the capture type is set to Image Capture (see ["Setting the Video or Screen Capture Type"](#target9000071)).
