The Respiration Gating status for frames and contours is also available in Review Mode.

# EP Recording System Interface

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/AbouttheCardioLabInterface\_EN.xml

The EP Recording System Interface serves as an interface between the CARTO™ 3 System and compatible EP recording systems. The end result is enhanced functionality and easier cabling between the two systems.

## Data Connectivity for EP Recording Systems

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/EP RECORDING SYSTEMS INTERFACE\_EN.xml

Interfacing with EP Recording systems includes both hardware and data connectivity. This chapter describes only data connectivity.

The CARTO™ 3 System provides an interface that supports data connectivity with the EP recording system. This interface can be used for a variety of features, based on the CARTO™ 3 System configuration and the functionality that was implemented on the recording system.

Following are the main supported features:

* Send events, such as point acquisition or the end of stimulation, from the CARTO™ 3 System to the recording system
* Send images to the recording system (for example, Map images)
* Send video captures to the recording system (CardioLab® only)
* Import the patient details from the recording system
* Synchronize the view between the two systems
* Synchronize the stimulation routing between the two systems

For details, refer to the *Connectivity User Guide*.

### Point Data

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/DataHandling\_3-2\_EN.xml

The EP Recording System Interface enables the recording system to automatically record events from the CARTO™ 3 System.

Patient, study, and point data are internally assigned unique identifiers. Whenever data is accessed on either system, these identifiers are checked to verify that they match. If for any reason they do not match, the requested operation will not be performed and a message appears advising you of the problem. This verification ensures that data integrity is maintained for all data shared between the systems.

When point data is interfaced, the following information is sent to the EP recording system:

* Time (this time is used as the event time on the EP recording system)
* Map Name
* LAT (ms)
* Unipolar and Bipolar Voltage (mV)
* Tag name and type applied to the point
* Short comment (up to 30 characters)

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Only 30 characters of text (including spaces) are sent to the EP recording system. This applies to the Map Name and comments in the Point List. |

The following commands have a unique behavior when the EP Recording System Interface is in use.

Table 52: Remap and Save Map As Functionality with the EP Recording System Interface

| Command | Behavior | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Remap | Baseline points of the map are not sent as point events to the EP recording system, regardless of the communication state. | Points acquired after remapping are marked as interfaced points. |
| Save Map As | If the systems are in an interfaced communication state (Active or Passive), all points of the new map are interfaced to new point events on the EP recording system. | If the systems are not in an interfaced communication state, the new map cannot be synchronized with the EP recording system. |

### End Stimulation Data

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Stimulation\_log\_c3\_EN.xml

When the CARTO™ 3 System detects a stimulation sequence having at least four stimulation pulses on the stimulation train, the CARTO™ 3 System monitors the data and calculates the stimulation parameters. At the end of the stimulation sequence, the following data can be sent to the EP recording system:

* Stimulation electrode pairs
* Pacing Cycle Length (PCL)
* Last five extra-stimulus intervals

The CARTO™ 3 System uses a configurable time interval (Post-stimulation Detection Interval Criterion) to determine the end of stimulation.[[25]](#footnote-25) If a stimulation train is present and no stimulation pulse is detected during the interval, stimulation is considered to be over.

To set this interval, click ECG > Stimulation End Configuration.

You can disable recording system notification for specific stimulation input (recording system, PIU, or both).

### Changes to the User Interface

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ChangestotheUserInterface\_c3-2\_EN.xml

When working with the Interface, you must select the correct recording system in the Connected Machines section of the Study Setup screen.

When communication with the EP recording system is detected (Standby communication state), several previously disabled options are now enabled.

* The Import button is enabled in the Patient List dialog box.
* The Communication State icon displays the current state, and can be used to toggle between Passive Interface and Active Interface states.
* The Tools > EP Recording menu commands are enabled (see ["EP Recording Submenu"](#target9000082))
* Interfaced points are indicated in the Point List.

### EP Recording System Interface Preferences

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/EPRS\_preferences\_c3-2\_EN.xml

The EP Recording Preferences dialog box is used to specify the information that is sent to the EP recording system when the EP Recording System Interface is in use. The options are described in the following table (see [Table 53: "Commands in the EP Recording System Sending Preferences Dialog Box"](#target9000083)).

![eprs_preferences_db_v3-2.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 133: EP Recording Preferences Dialog Box

Table 53: Commands in the EP Recording System Sending Preferences Dialog Box

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Ablation points | When selected, points acquired during application of RF energy are sent to the EP recording system. |
| Stimulation event through CARTO™ 3 System | When selected, an event is sent to the recording system when stimulation is applied from the CARTO™ 3 System.  This option is not available when pacing through the direct stimulation ports. |
| Stimulation event through recording system (where available) | When selected, the CARTO™ 3 System sends a stimulation event to the recording system when stimulation routing was set from the recording system. |
| Snapshots | When selected, all screen captures taken in the CARTO™ 3 System are automatically sent to the recording system.  Snapshots are sent in JPG format. |
| Movies | When selected, all video captures taken in the CARTO™ 3 System are automatically sent to the recording system.  CardioLab® only. |

### EP Recording Submenu

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/eprs\_menu\_v3-2\_EN.xml

The Tools > EP Recording submenu is available when the EP Recording System Interface is enabled.

Table 54: Menu Options for the EP Recording System Interface

| Command | Function |
| --- | --- |
| Passive Interface | Activates the Passive Interface state |
| Active Interface | Activates the Active Interface state |
| Re-Synchronize | Resynchronizes interfaced point data to the EP recording system |
| Import Patient Data | Imports patient data (demographics) from the EP recording system |
| Preferences | Opens the EP Recording System Sending Preferences dialog box |

## Supported EP Recording Systems

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/EPRS\_spportedSystems\_c3\_EN.xml

The EP Recording System Interface supports the following recording systems:

* Bard LabSystem™ PRO with CLEARSIGN™ amplifier
* GE CardioLab® EP Recording System
* Siemens AXIOM Sensis XP

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  The EP Recording System Interface is an optional module in the recording system, and its functionality may change from one version to the other.  For details, contact your EP recording system representative. |

## Communication States

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/COMMUNICATION STATES\_EN.xml

When using the EP Recording System Interface, there are four possible states of communication between the two systems. Two states refer to physical connection. Two states are set in the software.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Disconnected | There is no communication between the two systems. |
| Standby | Bilateral communication has been established between the two systems. Only patient data (demographics on the recording system) can be imported from the recording system. |
| Passive Interfaced | When the two systems are in standby, the Passive Interfaced state is activated in the recording system. In this state, both systems are connected and share the same study information. The CARTO™ 3 System sends events to the recording system. The recording system stores these events and snapshots of maps created on the CARTO™ 3 System. |
| Active Interfaced | The Active Interfaced communication state is activated in the CARTO™ 3 System. In Active Interfaced state, selecting a point on the CARTO™ 3 System selects the corresponding event on the recording system, and vice versa. This is in addition to the functionality of the Passive Interfaced state. |

Points acquired in Passive Interfaced and Active Interfaced States are referred to as *interfaced points*.

In the software applications of both systems, icons are used to indicate the communication states.

Table 55: Icons for Communication States

| State | CARTO™ 3 System Icons | CardioLab Icons | AXIOM Sensis XP Icons | LabSystem™ PRO Icons |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Disconnected | ![EP_icon_disconnected.png](data:image/png;base64...) | ![disconnected_cl.png](data:image/png;base64...) | ![disconnected_sensis.png](data:image/png;base64...) | ![disconnected_bard.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| Standby | ![EP_icon_standby.png](data:image/png;base64...) | ![standby_cl.png](data:image/png;base64...) | ![standby_sensis.png](data:image/png;base64...) | ![standby_bard.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| Passive Interfaced | ![EP_icon_passiveInterfaced.png](data:image/png;base64...) | ![passsive_inter_cl.png](data:image/png;base64...) | ![passive_inter_sensis.png](data:image/png;base64...) | ![passsive_inter_bard.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| Active Interfaced | ![EP_icon_activeInterfaced.png](data:image/png;base64...) | ![active_inter_cl.png](data:image/png;base64...) | ![active_inter_sensis.png](data:image/png;base64...) | ![active_inter_bard.png](data:image/png;base64...) |

### Disconnected Communication State

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/WorkinginDisconnectedCommunicationState\_EN.xml

![EP_icon_disconnected.png](data:image/png;base64...)

The CARTO™ 3 System is in the Disconnected communication state in the following situations:

* The CARTO™ 3 System and the EP recording system are not physically connected.
* The EP recording system is turned off.

You may work with the CARTO™ 3 System in Disconnected state.

### Standby Communication State

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/WorkinginStandbyCommunicationState\_EN.xml

![EP_icon_standby.png](data:image/png;base64...)

The CARTO™ 3 System automatically enters Standby when communication is detected with the EP recording system.

Patient data can be imported during this state of operation.

Stimulation routing in the CARTO™ 3 System can be selected from the EP recording system in this state.[[26]](#footnote-26)

### Passive Interface Communication State

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/communicationStates\_interfaced\_c3\_EN.xml

![EP_icon_passiveInterfaced.png](data:image/png;base64...)

When you are working in the Passive Interface communication state, each point acquired on the CARTO™ 3 System corresponds to a unique event on the EP recording system.

Stimulation events can be sent during this state of operation.

The Passive Interface communication state is activated and deactivated from the EP recording system.

### Active Interface Communication State

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/communicationStates\_synchronized\_c3\_EN.xml

![EP_icon_activeInterfaced.png](data:image/png;base64...)

The Active Interface communication state enables synchronized display between the two systems. Selecting a point (or event) on one system automatically selects its corresponding point (or event) on the other system.

Multiple selection is not supported.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  When the systems are synchronized, selecting a point on the EP recording system will select the corresponding point on the CARTO™ 3 System. |

### Changing Communication State

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/EPRS\_switchEommunicationState\_c3\_EN.xml

Before changing the communication state, the systems must be connected.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | From the EP recording system, enter the Interfaced state.  The Communication State icon is updated on both systems. |
| 2. | To enter the Active Interface communication state, select Tools > EP Recording > Active Interface.  The Communication State icon is updated on both systems. |
| 3. | To return to the Passive Interface state, select Tools > EP Recording > Passive Interface. |
| 4. | To return to the Standby state, exit the Interfaced state **from the EP recording system**. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  You can also switch between Passive Interface and Active Interface states by clicking the Communication State icon.  ![interfaceState_list_c3.png](data:image/png;base64...) |

## Importing Patient Data from the EP Recording System

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ImportingPatientDatafromtheCardioLabSystem\_c3\_EN.xml

Certain patient data (demographics) entered on the EP recording system can be imported to the CARTO™ 3 System. Data may be imported in any communication state except Disconnected. The following patient data is imported:

* Last name
* First name
* ID number
* Date of birth
* Gender

### Considerations When Using an EP Recording System with the CARTO™ 3 System

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/EP\_RecordingSystem\_considerations\_c3\_EN.xml

**Patient Data:**

The EP Recording System and the CARTO™ 3 System may support different character sets for entering patient data. When setting up a study:

VSTS: 35936

* Enter the patient data on the EP Recording System, and import the data in the CARTO™ 3 System (from the Study Setup screen, click Patient List > Import).
* Do not leave the Gender field blank.

If it is blank, Gender will be randomly set in the CARTO™ 3 System.

* Only Latin letters, numbers, dashes, and underscores are supported by the CARTO™ 3 System.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If other characters are used in the EP recording system, the Patient Data will remain blank after importing. If this occurs, edit the data in the EP recording system and import it again. |

**Map Names:**

Use names with less than 30 characters.

**Number of Points:**

Some recording systems limit interfaced points in a Study. If you acquire more than the limit, the additional events may not appear in the recording system.

### Importing Patient Data to the CARTO™ 3 System

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/EPRS\_ImportPatientData\_toCarto\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Begin a new study on both systems. |
| 2. | Enter patient data on the EP recording system. |
| 3. | In the CARTO™ 3 System, go to the Study Setup guide and click Patient List.  The Import button in the Patient List dialog box is enabled (see [Figure 134: "Patient List Dialog Box when the EP Recording System Interface Is Installed"](#target9000084)). |
| 4. | Click Import. |

Patient data is imported to the appropriate fields.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE   * If you already entered data in the Study Setup guide, or if a file was already created on the CARTO™ 3 System with the same patient name, you will be asked to confirm overwriting that data. * If patient demographics were not entered on the EP recording system, a message appears advising you that patient data cannot be imported. |

Data not entered on the EP recording system can be entered manually on the CARTO™ 3 System.

![EP_patientList_db.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 134: Patient List Dialog Box when the EP Recording System Interface Is Installed

### Updating Patient Data That Was Modified on the EP Recording System

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/EPRS\_UpdatePatientData\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Enter Standby or one of the interfaced communication states. |
| 2. | Click Study > Edit Patient Details.  The Edit Patient Details dialog box appears. |
| 3. | Click Patient List. |
| 4. | Click Import.  You will be asked to confirm overwriting existing data. |

The patient data on the CARTO™ 3 System is updated with any changes made on the EP recording system.

## Point Data with the EP Recording System Interface

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/WorkingwithPointData\_c3\_EN.xml

When the systems are in an interfaced state, acquiring a point in the CARTO™ 3 System results in the creation of an equivalent event on the EP recording system. The point event is given the same ID# that was assigned in the Point List.

These interfaced points are marked by a checkmark in the Int. column of the Point List.

![EP_pointList_interfacedPts_c3.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 135: Interfaced Points in the Point List

### Selecting Points

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/SelectingPoints\_EN.xml

You can select a point on the CARTO™ 3 System in any state of operation by clicking the point in the Point List or on a map.

In Active Interface state, selecting an interfaced point on the CARTO™ 3 System automatically selects the equivalent point event on the EP recording system.

### Editing Point and Map Data in Interfaced Communication States

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/EditingPointandMapDatainInterfacedCommunicationStates\_EN.xml

When you edit interfaced points on the CARTO™ 3 System, the equivalent event is updated on the EP recording system. All editing functions can be performed as usual, including:

* Editing the point annotation
* Moving points to a different map
* Copying points to a different map
* Deleting points from a map
* Restoring points to a map
* Renaming maps

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  When working in Active Interface communication state, selecting a point on the EP recording system will select the corresponding point on the CARTO™ 3 System. |

### Editing or Acquiring Point Data in Disconnected or Standby Communication States

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/EditingAcquiringPointDatainDisconnectedorStandbyCommunicationStates\_EN.xml

You can work with a CARTO™ 3 Study in Disconnected or Standby State. However, new point data acquired in these states will not be transferred to the EP recording system.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Points acquired when the systems are Disconnected or in Standby ***cannot*** be synchronized to point events on the EP recording system afterwards. |

### Synchronizing Point Data

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/SynchronizingPointData\_EN.xml

If you begin an interfaced CARTO™ 3 Study and the systems become disconnected, edited data of interfaced points can be resynchronized when the systems are reconnected. This enables updating any changes made to already interfaced points.

#### Resynchronizing a Study on Both Systems

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/EPRS\_Resynchronize\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Enter Passive Interfaced or Active Interfaced communication state. |
| 2. | Load the same study on both systems. |
| 3. | Click Tools > EP Recording > Re-Synchronize. |

Changes to previously interfaced points (deletions, edited annotations, etc.), are updated on the EP recording system.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  The following changes are not synchronized with events on the EP recording system, if they were performed outside of an interfaced state of communication:   * Copying of points to a different map * Save Map As * Restore from the Recycle Bin |

## Extended Functionality

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/eprs\_advancedStimulationFeatures\_c3\_EN.xml

The Bard LabSystem PRO Recording System supports an extended interface with the CARTO™ 3 System which enables additional functionality.[[27]](#footnote-27)

When this extended interface is in use, stimulation routing in the CARTO™ 3 System can be selected by the user from the EP recording system. The CARTO™ 3 System behaves as if stimulation routing was performed through its User Interface. The stimulation routing indication on the CARTO™ 3 System reflects the new routing condition, and the EP recording system is informed about this condition as well.

When stimulation is detected, an event is sent to the EP recording system at the end of the stimulation. This event contains data relevant to the detected stimulation. (When the extended interface is not in use, this notification is sent as a point event with a stimulation comment.)

For details, refer to the documentation provided with the recording system.

# Advanced Reference Annotation (ARA)

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/signalAnotation\_title\_v7\_EN.xml

## ARA Overview

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/signalAnotation\_AdvancedReferenceIntro\_v7\_EN.xml

**ARA** (Advanced Reference Annotation) is a reference annotation algorithm that uses up to 5 intracardiac bipolar channels of the reference catheter and body surface ECG channels to define the reference timing. The algorithm, which uses the signal's center of energy for reference annotation calculation, dynamically evaluates the ECG channels to eliminate channels with noise and automatically determines both atrial activation time and ventricular activation time.

The dynamic channel selection and differentiation between atrial and ventricular activity allows for detection and repeatability of reference annotations in complex arrhythmias.

The ARA algorithm has the following modes for calculating reference annotations:

* Automatically selects multiple bipolar intracardiac (IC) channels (up to 5 of the most distal) of the selected reference catheter for atrial cases
* Any single user-defined IC bipolar channel of the selected reference catheter for atrial cases
* Body surface ECG precordial channels (V1-V6)

REP9626 1.1.7, V7 ARA

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE   * Using the ARA algorithm for the following clinical scenarios may result in decreased performance:   + Atrial Fibrillation   + AVNRT or accessory pathway arrhythmias (WPW) with an IC reference   + An IC catheter in the ventricle as a ventricular reference   + When atrial activation constantly coincides with the QRS complex with an IC reference * In these cases, it is recommended to use one of the alternative Reference Criteria provided by the CARTO™ 3 System. * The CARTOUNIVU™ Module Cine mode cannot be used with ARA. |

## Performing a Study with ARA

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ARAFeature\_PerformingStudyARA-TopLevelHeading\_EN.xml

Verify the following before activating ARA:

* The body surface ECG patches (precordial leads) are placed on the patient and connected to the CARTO™ 3 System.

This is necessary because all IC annotations are checked against the precordial leads to filter out ventricular activations from an atrial map.

* When working with IC ARA, before acquiring an ULS contour or EA points (either continuous or manual), ensure that a physically stable catheter (desired to be the reference catheter) is connected to the PIU and selected in the Map Setup screen (for example, CS).

REP9626 1.1.4, V7 ARA

* There must not be significant noise on the reference catheter signals. The ARA algorithm only uses signals with good signal-to-noise ratio for reference annotation calculation.

### Activating ARA

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ARAFeature\_ActivatingARA\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | In the Map Setup screen, select the Mapping Catheter from the drop-down list. |
| 2. | Select the Chamber of Interest from the drop-down list. |
| 3. | Set the Reference Criterion to Automatic.  This selection ensures that ARA is the reference annotation algorithm used in the map.   |  |  | | --- | --- | | ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Automatic is the default Reference Criterion for the following Study Templates: Atrial Flutter, Focal Atrial Tachycardia, Ventricular Tachycardia, and Other. | |
| 4. | Select the proper Reference Channels according to the Chamber of Interest.  You can select channels for any catheter defined by the template, whether connected or disconnected (shadowed), except for the mapping catheter.   |  |  | | --- | --- | | Atrium | Multi-channel: Select REF All for the Channels of the IC reference catheter, or select BS V1 - V6.  ![ARAFeature_MapSetup_Multi-channel_Atrium.png](data:image/png;base64...)  For IC reference: The 5 most distal paired bipolar IC channels of the reference catheter are evaluated by the algorithm for use as references. (In the example above: REF1-2, REF3-4, REF5-6, REF7-8, REF9-10.) | | Atrium | Single channel: Select a single bipolar IC channel from the selected reference catheter's available channels.  ![ARAFeature_MapSetup_SingleChannel_Atrium.png](data:image/png;base64...) | | Ventricle | Multi-channel: BS V1-V6 is automatically selected and displayed in gray as Channels to be used as the reference.  ![ARA_MapSetup_Ventricle.png](data:image/png;base64...) | |
| 5. | (Optional) Select the desired color for the reference annotations. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  There is an annotation latency when using the ARA algorithm due to annotation calculation time. The dashed line of the ARA reference annotation in the Monitor pane may be displayed up to 850 ms after signal display in the Monitor pane. |

### ARA Algorithm Training Period

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ARAFeature\_AlgorithmLearning\_EN.xml

A training period is required for the ARA algorithm to evaluate ECG data and to analyze cardiac activity and environmental noise on the signals. During the training period, the ARA algorithm selects the channels to be used for annotation calculations, an alert appears, and continuous acquisition is disabled. After the training period, the channels to be used for annotation calculations are selected, reference annotations appear, and acquisition is enabled.

The ARA algorithm training period differs, depending on the Reference Channels used in the study:

* Body surface- 9 seconds
* IC- 18 seconds

If this ECG data is not available (for example, if the catheter was disconnected and reconnected), the system waits until the data is accumulated. An alert appears and continuous acquisition is disabled.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Reconnecting the reference catheter during the training period may trigger a re-training period. |

A training period is automatically activated in the following situations:

* Setting the Reference Criterion to Automatic, such as when creating a new map or during remap

The training is repeated every 5 seconds until points or contours are acquired.

* Changing the Reference Channels in an empty map (without points or contours)
* Starting continuous acquisition in an empty map (without points or contours)
* Manually acquiring the first electroanatomical point in a map
  + The point will be on the second annotated beat after clicking the acquisition button (due to training)
  + The beat buffer will be cleared, due to training. (Beats will start to accumulate after clicking the acquisition button, so 10 beats after clicking the acquisition button, the beat buffer will be full.)

When an EA point or an ULS contour is acquired, the selected IC channels used for ARA calculation for that map are fixed.

### User Interface

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ARAFeature\_UserInterface\_EN.xml

ARA data is displayed in the following ECG viewers:

* Annotation Viewer
* Baseline Point Viewer
* Monitor
* Pattern Bank
* Selected Point Viewer

When single-channel ARA is used:

* The reference annotations are displayed as single dots, colored according to the Reference Color setting in the Map Setup screen, in all relevant ECG viewers.
* The selected bipolar channel is displayed with an R label in all ECG viewers.
* The reference channel is displayed by default in all ECG viewers.

![ARA_Single-channel_Atrium_SPV_Dot.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 136: Single-channel ARA Reference Annotations- Selected Point Viewer

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  When using ARA, the algorithm uses the signal's center of energy for reference annotation calculation. Therefore, the reference annotation will not always be in a consistent fixed position, such as the maximum value. If you suspect that a reference annotation is inaccurate, edit the reference annotation or delete the corresponding point or points on the map. If you suspect that many reference annotations are inaccurate, start a new map. |

When multi-channel ARA is used:

* The reference annotations are displayed as dashed lines in the ECG viewers, colored according to the Reference Color setting in the Map Setup screen, in all relevant ECG viewers.
* The channels that are used for reference annotation calculation are displayed with an R label in the ECG viewers.
  + In atrial studies, the 5 most distal bipolar channels of the selected catheter are evaluated by the algorithm. The channels that are selected for reference annotation calculation are displayed with R labels and may be a subset of the 5, depending on their quality to serve as good reference channels. When an EA point or an ultrasound contour is acquired, the selected IC channels used for ARA calculation for that map are fixed.
  + When using body surface ECG reference channels, BS V1-V6 channels are displayed with R labels and evaluated by the algorithm with each annotation.
* The system doesn't automatically add reference signals to the display in ECG viewers (to prevent overcrowding of the signal display).

You can manually add channels to the display.

![ARA_Multi-channel_Atrium_SPV.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 137: Multi-channel ARA in the Atrium- Selected Point Viewer

![ARA_VENTRICLE_MAPPING_SPV.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 138: Multi-channel ARA in the Ventricle- Selected Point Viewer

When ARA is selected, the Ultrasound window and pattern viewers (Pattern Matching Viewer, Pattern Bank, etc.) display the following:

* Body surface ECG reference channel: V5 precordial lead
* Multi-channel IC reference: The most distal bipolar channel evaluated for reference annotation calculation for that map (i.e., the most distal of the map's fixed channels)
* Single-channel IC reference: Selected bipolar reference channel

![ARA_ULSViewer_Ventricle.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 139: Ultrasound Window: Body Surface ECG Reference Channel

![ARA_ULSViewer_Atrium.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 140: Ultrasound Window: Atrial Mapping

![ara_pattern_viewer.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 141: Pattern Matching Viewer with ARA

### Functionality When No Reference Annotations are Detected

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ARAFeature\_NoReferenceAnnotations\_EN.xml

There are several conditions where reference annotations are not detected. If an alert appears that no reference annotations are detected, continuous acquisition is disabled.

The system adds a visual indication by the Acquisition Panel.

![ARAFeature_AcquisitionPanel_NoRefAnnotation.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 142: No Reference Annotation Indication

Manual point acquisition is still possible.

* The acquired point type is Location Only.
* The acquired point does not have a LAT value. Edit the point manually to set a LAT value.
* The acquired point has a comment in the Point List: "No Reference Annotation Point".
* If the point is acquired during ablation, it receives the appropriate ablation tag (according to the ablation mode).

Annotation Viewer display is continuously updated, but without any reference or Window of Interest (WOI) lines.

CARTOSOUND™ Module: The ultrasound fan is not displayed in the Ultrasound window. The SOUNDSTAR™ Catheter visualization is not displayed in the map viewer.

### Copying or Moving Points between Maps

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ARAFeature\_CopyMovingPoints\_EN.xml

You can copy or move points from maps with ARA (all types) to maps with any other reference annotation, including ARA. You cannot copy points from maps with a different reference annotation algorithm to maps with ARA.

ARA to ARA copy or move must be between maps with the same ARA type:

* Multi-channel BS to multi-channel BS
* Single-channel IC to single-channel IC (same channel)
* Multi-channel IC to multi-channel IC (same reference catheter)

When copying or moving points from maps with ARA to maps with a different reference annotation algorithm or from maps with ARA to maps with ARA, the system automatically adjusts the reference annotations according to the target map's reference annotation algorithm. This adjustment also occurs when using the CARTOREPLAY™ Module to retroactively acquire ARA reference points.

In maps with multi-channel IC ARA, the algorithm selects which channels to take into consideration and requires a shared channel or channels used for reference detection between maps. When copying or moving from multi-channel IC ARA to multi-channel IC ARA, a point will not be copied or moved if the destination map does not share any reference channel with the target map.

### ARA Behavior when Pacing is Active

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ARAFeature\_PacingBehavior\_EN.xml

The ARA reference annotation algorithm detects when pacing is performed via a catheter connected to the CARTO™ 3 System. ARA differentiates between pacing from the Reference Channels and pacing from catheters whose channels are not Reference Channels:

* For pacing from a Reference Channel, the reference annotation is located on the pacing spike.

The pacing spike is followed by a blanking period of 175 ms on the reference channels. No annotations are issued during the blanking period.

* For pacing from a catheter connected to the CARTO™ 3 System, whose channels are not Reference Channels, possible pacing artifacts are filtered out since they may conflict with chamber activation signals on the Reference Channels.

The pacing spike is filtered out by replacing it with a linear function, 7 ms before the spike and up to 35 ms after the spike.

* For pacing from a catheter that is not connected to the CARTO™ 3 System, such as a pacemaker or a catheter connected to the EP recording system, the pacing artifact is not removed by the algorithm, and it may lead to an inconsistent reference annotation.
* During pacing, a pacing artifact appears on the signals shortly after the last pacing spike (around 2 seconds). In rare cases, when using high power pacing, the presence of the pacing artifact may cause the ARA algorithm to temporarily miss annotations for a few beats following the pacing artifact.

REP10631\_1.0, Section 5.1.4.2

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  When multi-channel ARA is used, ensure that all pacing stimulation is routed through the CARTO™ 3 System. Stimulation that is not routed through the CARTO™ 3 System might cause inconsistency in the reference annotation and inaccuracies in the map. |

## Annual Service Activity

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/warranty\_preventiveMaintenance\_EN.xml

Biosense Webster, Inc. will provide annual service activity for the System at the customer’s site during the warranty period.[[28]](#footnote-28)

Following the warranty period, annual service activity is required to ensure proper functionality of the system, either in the framework of a Service Contract, or by setting an appointment with a Biosense Webster, Inc. Call Center.

Annual service activity will include any actions that Biosense Webster, Inc. considers necessary to ensure proper operation of the System. Annual service activity will be scheduled for normal business hours.

### Standard Periodic Maintenance Activities

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/warranty\_standardPeriodicMaintenance\_EN.xml

During the annual service visit, the Biosense Webster, Inc. service personnel will perform the following activities:

* Visual inspection of the system components for signs of physical damage
* Ensure that the components are assembled correctly (including checking for loose screws on the cart)
* Inspect cables and connectors, replacing cables if required
* Ensuring that the PIU is securely mounted on the table arm (if in use)
* Verify that serial numbers of system components match the records

### Overview of Acceptance Tests

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/warranty\_ATP\_outline\_user\_EN.xml

The Acceptance Tests Procedure (ATP) is a series of tests used to verify the integrity of the CARTO™ 3 and the system elements. These tests are performed after the System is installed or serviced. The acceptance tests are performed manually, using the CARTO™ 3 System Service Utility.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteCaution.png](data:image/png;base64...) | *CAUTION*  The acceptance tests are to be performed in the field only by trained and certified Biosense Webster, Inc. service personnel. |

The CARTO™ 3 System Service Utility is designed to test the CARTO™ 3 System and validate its readiness for clinical use. The validation is performed after various installation, service, upgrade, and preventive maintenance procedures. The utility is designed to run system functionality tests. Each test can be performed individually to validate specific functions of the CARTO™ 3 System.

At the end of each test, a detailed test report is generated.

Table 56: Functional Tests

| Function | Test | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Magnetic Tests** | | To verify the ability of the PIU to identify and accurately measure magnetic fields |
|  | Cube Test | To verify system integrity and functionality after installation  The CARTO™ 3 System mapping mechanism must be capable of measuring a known volume based on the magnetic field contained within it |
| Relative and Cloud Test | To verify system integrity and functionality after installation  The CARTO™ 3 System magnetic location mechanism must be capable of measuring a known repeatable position of each magnetic sensor based on the magnetic field contained within it |
| **Advanced Catheter Location (ACL) Tests** | | To verify the functionality of the CARTO™ 3 System Advanced Catheter Location (ACL) technology |
|  | Raw Electrode Currents Test |
| Calibration Test |
| **DC Test** | | To ensure that there is no difference of potentials between the catheter electrodes |
| **Resistance Test** | | To check the proper functionality of the RL lead circuitry |
| **ECG Tests** | | To verify ECG functionality of the CARTO™ 3 System |
|  | Amplitude Test | To verify that output measurements of all channels meet the manufacturer specifications |
| Limb Lead Test | To verify that functionality of filter and amplifiers of all channels meet the manufacturer specifications |
| Noise Test | To measure the noise level on each of the 90 ECG channels, while all of them are grounded to the RL |
| Pacing Test | To verify proper hardware functionality while pacing is switched between different channels |
| **Source to Intensifier distance (SID) Measurements** | | To determine the effect of magnet distortion of the fluoroscope in different positions |
| **CARTOUNIVU™ Module Test** | | To verify functionality of the CARTOUNIVU™ Module (if installed) |

# LAT Velocity Vectors

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/LVV\_v8\_EN.xml

The LAT Velocity Vectors (LVV) feature provides a representation of the activation wave propagation over a substrate map done in sinus rhythm or during pacing. The vectors indicate the LAT movement on the 3D map using the anatomy and point information of an existing LAT map. The vectors appear thicker and shorter for areas of relatively slower conduction (below the user defined threshold), and thinner and longer for areas of relatively normal or fast conduction (above the user-defined threshold).

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  The LAT Velocity Vectors feature was validated on ventricular maps created during sinus rhythm or pacing, and not during tachycardia. Use of the algorithm was validated only in these conditions. |

The LAT velocity vectors are calculated while considering LAT values of the near surroundings of the vector location, and therefore you do not need a full map of the chamber for the vectors to be calculated and displayed. However, the area where the vectors are displayed must be dense enough to allow continuous interpolation of activation between the points. If the map is not dense enough, vectors are not displayed.

The algorithm processes the data as follows:

* For each voxel (3D pixel) on the reconstruction, the system analyzes the LAT values and distances of the projected points that are in the vicinity of the voxel.
* Where enough data exists, the System employs a statistical algorithm to determine the conduction's direction and speed at this region by means of a velocity vector.
* It takes into account LAT values suggestive of wave discontinuation (velocity below 0.1 mm/ms).
* Each vector is assigned with
  + A **direction** — represented by an arrow
  + A **velocity value** — measured in mm/ms, used with the velocity threshold setting
  + A **LAT value** — used for LAT velocity vector clustering and propagation display

LVV is not supported for COHERENT maps.

![LVV_LATMapWithLVV.PNG](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 143: LAT Map with LAT Velocity Vectors

## Viewing LAT Velocity Vectors

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/LVV\_Viewing\_LVV\_EN.xml

To activate the feature and adjust the display settings, right-click the map and select LAT Vector Setup.

![LVV_LAT_vector_setup_window.PNG](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 144: LAT Vector Setup Window

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Show Conduction Velocity Vectors | When selected, velocity vectors are displayed.  There may be a slight delay while the vectors are calculated. |
| Static | When selected, the velocity vectors are shown as static. |
| Dynamic | When selected, the velocity vectors are animated. |
| Intensify Slow Vectors | When selected, velocity vectors with speed above the user-defined threshold are dimmed. |
| Vector Clustering | LVV vector clustering is designed to visually emphasize the vectors' main direction and areas with potential discontinuity.  The default value is 4.0.  When vector clustering is changed, the system re-analyzes each vector's LAT values and the distances between the evaluated vector and other vectors in its vicinity. The evaluated vector's location and angle are adjusted towards the center of the analyzed area.  If the vector's distance movement exceeds 4 mm, or orientation change exceeds 45 degrees, the vector will not be displayed on the map for that clustering level.  See [Figure 146: "Sample Map with Lower (Left) and Higher (Right) Vector Clustering"](#target9000086) |
| LAT Velocity Threshold | User-defined threshold for velocity value. Ranges between 0.05 to 1 mm/ms. The default value is 0.20 mm/ms.  Vectors below the selected threshold are thicker and shorter than the ones above the threshold. |
| Do not show this window when re-calculation is required | When enabled, the LAT Vector Setup window will not pop up when changes are made to the map that causes the Velocity Vectors to disappear. |

LAT Velocity Vectors are calculated separately for each map. They can be displayed in either map viewer.

REP14517 A-4

You can display LAT Velocity Vectors for multiple maps in the same map viewer. Vectors in the active map are presented with normal display settings. Vectors in the additional maps are presented in a different color.

![LVV_sample_map_with_intensify_slow_vectors.PNG](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 145: Sample Map with Intensify Slow Vectors Off (Left) and Active (Right)

![LVV_sample_map_with_CompressionLevels.PNG](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 146: Sample Map with Lower (Left) and Higher (Right) Vector Clustering

## Recalculating Velocity Vectors

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/LVV\_RecalculatingVelocityVectors\_EN.xml

REP14517 D-4

If the map changes after the LAT Velocity Vectors have been calculated, either from a change to the FAM reconstruction or after adding, removing, or editing contributing points, the velocity vectors are no longer valid and are removed from the map. The LAT Vector Setup window appears so you can recalculate the LAT Velocity Vectors and display them again.

To recalculate the LAT Velocity Vectors, select the Show Conduction Velocity Vectors checkbox.

If you don't want the window to appear when recalculation is necessary, select the Do not show this window when re-calculation is required checkbox.

## Propagation Maps with LAT Velocity Vectors

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/LVV\_PropagationMapsWithLVV\_EN.xml

Each of the Velocity Vectors is associated with a LAT value (this is only used by the algorithm) that represents the time that the wave passes in the vicinity of the vector . To further investigate the activation sequence, the LVV can be played as a propagation wave on top of displayed vectors.

During playback, the propagation wave is white over the vectors, which are displayed in gray.

![LVV_SamplePropagationMap.PNG](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 147: Images of LAT Velocity Vectors in a Sample Propagation Map

## Different Types of Velocity Vectors

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/LVV\_InteractionCoherentMapping\_EN.xml

The LVV feature provides a local solution for calculating velocities, whereas COHERENT maps provide a global fit solution.

The two types of vectors cannot be active in the same map viewer simultaneously. However, they can be displayed side by side, with one in the Main Map Viewer and the other in the Additional Map Viewer.

If you change the vector type for a map, the newly selected vector type is displayed, and the former is removed. The vector type is indicated under the color scale bar.

![LVV_LAT_ColorScale.PNG](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 148: LAT Color Scale Bar with COHERENT Vectors (Left) and LAT Vectors (right)

**Display behavior with COHERENT map:**

* LVV cannot be displayed over a COHERENT map.
* If a COHERENT map is displayed, LVV are removed, and the LVV option is disabled in the map viewer menu.
* After returning from a COHERENT map, the LVV will automatically re-appear (unless COHERENT vectors are displayed).

# HD Catheter Support

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HD\_catheters\_intro\_8p3\_EN.xml

## Overview

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HD\_catheters\_overview\_8p3\_EN.xml

The CARTO™ 3 system supports connection of diagnostic catheters with more than 20 mapping electrodes to theCARTO™ 3 System. These catheters are referred to as HD catheters.[[29]](#footnote-29) These catheters connect to the CARTO™ 3 PIU via the Signal Processing Unit (SPU).

HD Catheters can be used for standard functionality, including mapping (both FAM and EA) and pacing. Bipolar channels between splines are not supported.

The HD Catheters can be used with most modules, with the following exceptions:

* Pairing visualization of HD catheters with the CARTO VIZIGO™ Sheath is not supported.
* CARTOFINDER™ Module is not supported with OPTRELL™ Catheters (part of the CARTO PRIME™ Module).
* Multipolar Mapping is not supported with the OCTARAY™ Catheter (part of the CARTO ELEVATE™ Module[[30]](#footnote-30)).

REP13141 D-2, D-3

REP11406 D-62, D-63, A-96, D-97, D-98

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  While mapping and before applying energy for ablation, check the catheter tip position in relation to the map. Use your common clinical practice for real-time verification of catheter locations throughout the procedure (inspection of IC signals, direct imaging guidance such as fluoroscopy or ultrasound, etc.). |

* Refer to the *Instructions for Use* (IFU) of the catheter before using this catheter with the CARTO™ 3 System.
* Refer to the *CARTO™ Signal Processing Unit (SPU) Hardware Instructions for Use* for details on connecting the SPU to the PIU.

### OCTARAY™ Catheters

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HD\_catheters\_OCTARAY\_8p3\_EN.xml

OCTARAY™ Catheters are multi-spline diagnostic catheters, available and supported in the following configurations (both D and F curve):

Table 57: OCTARAY™ Catheter Definitions

| Spline Length | # of Splines/Electrodes | Electrode Spacing |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2 cm | 8/51 | 3-3-3-3-3 mm |
| 2 cm | 8/51 | 2-5-2-5-2 mm |
| 1.5 cm | 8/51 | 2-2-2-2-2 mm |

The CARTO™ System supports 48 unipolar ECG channels and 40 adjacent bipolar channels along the splines.

### OPTRELL™ 48 Mapping Catheter

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/OPTRELL\_8p0\_Catheter\_Specifications\_EN.xml

The OPTRELL™ 48 Mapping Catheter is a triple-loop diagnostic catheter, with 2.4 × 2.4 spacing (in spline and across spline). It is available and supported in both D-F and F-J curves.

The CARTO™ System supports 48 unipolar ECG channels and 42 adjacent bipolar channels along the splines.

### OPTRELL™ 36 Mapping Catheters

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/OPTRELL\_36\_Catheter\_Specifications\_7p5\_EN.xml

The OPTRELL™ 36 Mapping Catheter is a triple-loop diagnostic catheter, with 2.4 × 2.4 spacing (in spline and across spline). It is available and supported in both D-F and F-J curves.

The CARTO™ System supports 36 unipolar ECG channels and 30 adjacent bipolar channels.

## CARTO™ Signal Processing Unit (SPU)

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HD\_catheters\_SPU\_intro\_EN.xml

The SPU is used for connecting HD catheters to the PIU.

![SPU_holder_veiw.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 149: SPU in Holder

For details regarding connection and care of the SPU, refer to the *CARTO™ Signal Processing Unit (SPU) Hardware Instructions for Use*.

### Connecting a HD Catheter to the SPU

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HD\_Catheters\_connecting\_to\_SPU\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Before connecting the catheter to the SPU, ensure that the SPU is connected and ready for use. The LED is green and the green connection icon appears on the System status bar. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Connect the catheter to the SPU extension cable connector.![OCTARAY_SPU_Extension_Cable.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 2. | Connect the SPU extension cable to the SPU. ![OCTARAY_SPU_and_Extension_Cable.png](data:image/png;base64...) |

The catheter is now connected to the SPU through the SPU extension cable connector.

### Status Indication Panel

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/SPU\_status\_indication\_v7p5\_EN.xml

When you create a new study with the SPU connected to the PIU, the SPU Status icon appears on the System Status Panel.

![SPU_Status_Panel_with_Fully_Connected_SPU_Highlighted_EN.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 150: Sample System Status Panel with Fully Connected SPU

The SPU automatically performs several start-up and validation functions, including electrical and external device communication status, cable connectivity, noise filtering, and temperature monitoring. If issues are detected, the SPU Status icon and/or the System messages provide indication.

Table 58: SPU Status Indication

| Status | Icon | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Connected | ![SPU_Icon_Indicator_Con_Con1_EN.png](data:image/png;base64...) | The SPU is fully connected and ready for use. This indicates the following:   * The branched cable is connected to the Power Supply. * The 20 POLE interface cables are connected to the SPU, and to the 20 POLE A and 20 POLE B sockets of the PIU. * A functioning Ethernet cable is connected to the SPU and the PIU. * The SPU and the PIU are synchronized. |
| Disconnected or Error | ![SPU_Icon_Indicator_Dis_Dis1_EN.png](data:image/png;base64...) | There is an error or a lack of communication between the SPU and the Workstation. |
| REP9626: V7 SPU 1.6.4, 1.8.2, 1.10.1  No Sync  or  Self test | ![SPU_Icon_Indicator_Dis_Con1_EN.png](data:image/png;base64...) | The SPU and the PIU are connected via the Ethernet cable, but they are not synchronized or when the SPU is first started, during the self-test. |
| Error 401  Map points cannot be acquired  This situation may be related to a buildup of electrostatic discharge. | ![SPU_Icon_Indicator_Dis_Con1_EN.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Ensure that the relative humidity in the operating room is between 25% and 75%. The System will return to a synchronized state and the System Sync icon turns green. |
| Error 401  Map points cannot be acquired  In rare situations:   * Voltage interruptions may occur that cut the power to the SPU * Out of range voltage may be supplied that results in the SPU automatically turning itself off   As a result, the SPU LED indicators turn off. | ![SPU_Icon_Indicator_Dis_Dis1_EN.png](data:image/png;base64...) | No action required.  Once the nominal voltage is restored, the SPU essential performance is restored and is safe to use. |

### SPU Troubleshooting

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/SPU\_troubleshooting\_IFU-cug\_7p5\_EN.xml

If an electrode is defective or in the sheath, there is no indication on the non-ACL electrodes. To determine if there is an issue with an electrode:

* Display all the unipolar signals in an ECG viewer.
* Inspect the signal quality of each electrode.
  + If the electrode is defective, the signal shown is random noise.
  + If the electrode is functional, but still in the sheath, the signal is flat.

## CARTO™ 3 System Interface with HD Catheters

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HD\_catheters\_interface\_EN.xml

![OPTRELL_GUI.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 151: Example: CARTO™ 3 Study with the OPTRELL™ Mapping Catheter

### Catheter Setup Screen

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HD\_catheters\_CatheterSetup\_EN.xml

When an HD catheter is connected to the SPU and/or defined in the template, both 20A and 20B sockets are displayed under the catheter image in the Catheter Setup screen. The label for the socket is automatically set to SPU.

You can change the text of the label for the socket, as well as other visualization settings for the HD catheter. For example, in the Color Per Spline section, set the spline color for the HD catheter to a single color for all splines, or choose to display a different color for each spline.

To change the orientation of the catheter image, center-click the image and rotate.

![C3_v7p5_optrell_Catheter_Setup_screen.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 152: Catheter Setup Screen with OPTRELL™ 48 Mapping Catheter Connected

If the HD catheter is connected to the SPU and/or defined in the template, both 20A and 20B sockets are displayed under the catheter image.

If the 20 POLE cables only partially connect the SPU to the PIU, the following is displayed on the Catheter Setup screen:

* The connected socket is blue
* The SPU image is shadowed
* No HD catheter image is displayed
* Socket label SPU is shadowed

### HD Catheter Visualization

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HD\_catheters\_visualization\_EN.xml

For optimal visualization of HD catheters:

* Ensure that the chest patches are positioned properly. They must be placed an adequate distance apart, and they must encircle the heart without overlapping. Use the Location Setup screen to assist in placing the patches in the outer part of the highlighted area.
* If possible, build the matrix with the catheter that will be used for mapping. Using a different catheter may affect visualization.

REP9626: V7 1.8.5 re: full cpm coverage

The System supports visualization of an extended shaft electrode (S3 proximal), in addition to the S1 distal electrode and the S2 electrode.

### Out of Sheath Indication for HD Catheters

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/SPU\_HD\_catheter\_out-of-sheath\_EN.xml

In situations where the catheter is partially out of the sheath but visualization is not possible, the tip of the shaft visualization is highlighted. This can occur when the catheter is first exiting the sheath, or in situations where conditions prevent visualization (for example, inadequate matrix). The indication disappears when the splines are visualized.

![in_sheath_7p5.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 153: HD Catheter with Out of Sheath Indication

This indication is on by default. It can be turned off and on in the Catheter Setup screen using the Show Out of Sheath Indication checkbox.

The catheter is available for mapping and building the matrix when the S1 electrode is out of the sheath.

### TRUEref™ Electrode Functionality

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/hd\_trueRef\_functionality\_EN.xml

REP9626: V7 1.8.8

Supported HD catheters have a special TRUEref™ electrode that can be used as a unipolar reference. This option is off by default when an HD catheter is connected. Unipolar channels of other catheters are not affected and continue to function according to the WCT or IC System setting.

To switch to using TRUEref™ as the unipolar reference, click the Enable TRUEref™ unipolar reference check box in the Filters Configuration dialog box.

![Trueref_Electrode_c7p3.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 154: Filters Configuration Dialog Box

You can change the unipolar reference for the HD Catheter in the same map, with no limitations.

If you start a CARTOFINDER™ recording session when TRUEref™ unipolar reference is active, the algorithm input will be based on the TRUEref™ unipolar signals.

If the TRUEref™ electrode is nonfunctional:

* If the HD Catheter is the mapping catheter, an error message is displayed, and no data can be acquired.
* If the HD Catheter is not the mapping catheter, an alert is displayed. The System will be able to acquire data with other catheters.

### SPU Smoothing Filter

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/SPU\_P3\_Smoothing\_Filter\_EN.xml

To select a Low Pass Filter that is optimal for ECG signals, select the Enable SPU smoothing filter from the Filter Configuration dialog box.

![Smoothing_filter_c7p3.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 155: Filter Configuration Dialog Box

This setting is saved with the Study and can also be saved to a template.

### TPI Functionality

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HD\_catheters\_TPI\_EN.xml

Tissue Proximity Indication (TPI) is active by default.

Table 59: TPI Functionality with Different HD Catheters

| Catheter | Functionality |
| --- | --- |
| OCTARAY™ Catheters | REP9626: V7 1.8.10  When the TPI filter is active:   * TPI functionality, including status indication and filtered point acquisition, is not available for the following electrodes:   B5, B6, C5, C6, E5, E6, F5, F6, G5, G6, H5, and H6   * If Distal Electrode or Either Electrode is selected, the CARTO™ 3 System does not acquire points for the following bipolar channels:   B5-6, C5-6, E5-6, F5-6, G5-6, and H5-6   * If Both Electrodes is selected, the CARTO™ 3 System does not acquire points for the following bipolar channels:   B4-B5, B5-6, C4-C5, C5-6, E4-E5, E5-6, F4-F5, F5-6, G4-G5, G5-6, H4-H5, and H5-6 |
| OPTRELL™ 48 Catheters | REP9626: V7 1.8.10 (From OCTARAY™)  TPI impedance calculation is not available for the following electrodes, as they are not directly connected to the PIU:   * A3, B3, C3, D3, E3, F3 * A6, B6, C6, D6, E6, F6   A *Virtual TPI* calculation is performed for these unsupported electrodes based on the TPI status of neighboring electrodes:  When both neighboring electrodes along the spline of any of the unsupported electrodes are in close proximity (positive TPI status), the unsupported electrode is considered positive as well. For example, if both electrodes A2 and A4 are positive, A3 is also considered to be positive.  Virtual TPI is automatically available when TPI is active and does not require any additional user configuration. Virtual TPI affects all TPI functionality, including status indication and filtered point acquisition. |
| OPTRELL™ 36 Catheters | TPI is available for all 36 electrodes. |

### Local Conduction Vectors (LCV)

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/OPTRELL\_LCV\_7p5\_EN.xml

OPTRELL™ Catheters only: Local Conduction Vectors (LCV) tool displays real-time information on conduction direction, propagation velocity, and low voltage areas for the OPTRELL™ Catheter 3D image.

The LCV feature is only available when mapping criterion is set to WaveFront or Late Annotation, and an LCV-supported catheter is set and configured as the mapping catheter.

To access the LCV settings, right-click the map and select Catheter Vector Setup. The Catheter Conduction Vector Setup window appears.

![Catheter_Conduction_Vector_Setup_1.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 156: Catheter Conduction Vector Setup

Table 60: Catheter Conduction Setup

| Setting | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Catheter Conduction Velocity Vectors | Visualization of local conduction vectors is enabled by default. Clear the checkbox to hide the vectors. This can be set for each map viewer.  The vectors appear thicker and shorter for areas of relative slow conduction, and thinner and longer for areas of relative fast/normal conduction. |
| Color | LAT (default): reflects the range of colors for the local conduction under the catheter surface from earliest to latest.  Solid: removes color, making arrows appear gray. |
| Size | needs FRS ref  Vector size scale values range from 1 to 11. Default is 5. |
| Bipolar scar threshold (mV) | A dark gray disk appears on top of the electrode when the catheter is in the proximity of a low voltage area and only while the electrode has positive tissue touch indication with TPI.  ![gray%20discs.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)  needs FRS ref  The default threshold is set to 0.15 mV.   |  |  | | --- | --- | | ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  This is not the same as the Bipolar scar threshold in the Scar Settings preferences. | |

There are 3 distinct velocity types: **slow conduction** area, **fast/normal conduction** area, **no conduction** (scar area):

![C3_V7p5_vectors_and_scar_indication.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 157: Vector Velocity Indication

When visualization of local conduction vectors is enabled, you can display vectors for the catheter shadow for a point. Right-click the map viewer background and select Show Catheter > LCV. The vectors appear on top of the catheter shadow, and the vectors appear less saturated.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  When choosing Show Catheter and editing a point, velocity vectors will be recalculated retrospectively based on the new parameters. |

Velocity vectors on the catheter shadow will change when point values are changed by:

* Reference annotation
* WOI borders
* VWOI borders
* Mapping annotation
* Late Boundary

You can display LCV on both map viewers, and on every map coloring type. They are also displayed on the following:

* Propagation/ Ripple display
* Glass mode
* Anatomical structure
* Merged images

## Pacing with HD Catheters

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/SPU\_ifu-cug\_pacing\_v7p5\_EN.xml

**Through the CARTO™ 3 System:**

Bipolar pacing on SPU electrodes is supported, except in the following situations:

* When the SPU is powered off.
* During PIU power-up initialization.
* When the PIU is powered off.
* When there is no communication with the Workstation.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE   * Do not disconnect the SPU during pacing. * If you disconnect the SPU and connect a different catheter to 20 POLE A, you must select the 20 POLE A channel for pacing, even if pacing was previously routed to SPU channels. The reverse also applies. |

**Through the Recording System:**

The SPU enables pacing through the Recording System. Pacing through the Recording System is also available:

* When the SPU is off.
* During PIU power-up initialization.

Each supported catheter has specific electrodes that are not available.

For additional information, refer to the relevant Catheter Support Module's Instructions for Use.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteCaution.png](data:image/png;base64...) | *CAUTION*  REP9626: V7 SPU 1.4.7, 1.4.8   * Do not pace through or connect channel 20 POLE A electrodes 17-20 to the Recording System.   REP9626: V7 SPU 1.4.9   * Do not connect channel 20 POLE A electrode 19 to the Recording System because you can lose the synchronization or sensing between the signals.   REP9626: V7 SPU 1.4.10   * Selecting channel 20 POLE A electrodes 19-20 (SPU Sync Signal) as the sensing channel in the pace stimulator, might result in non-synchronized pacing. |

BR#95035

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  When pacing starts, there might be some artifacts from power noise at the first pacing pulses. The artifacts will decay after the first few beats.  ![spu_pacing_artifacts.png](data:image/png;base64...) |

# CARTO™ 3 System with QDOT MICRO™ Module

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/QDOT\_title\_EN.xml

## Overview

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_intro\_8p1\_EN.xml

The **QDOT MICRO™ Module** provides real-time display of the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter tip surface temperature and microelectrode signals in the CARTO™ 3 System.  [[31]](#footnote-31)

QDOT MICRO™ Module features are available only with the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter. The QDOT MICRO™ Catheter has a force sensor,[[32]](#footnote-32) 6 thermocouples, and 3 microelectrodes.

When the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter is used with a compatible RF generator, support is available for both QMODE™ and QMODE+™ ablation modes.For more information about QMODE+™ refer to the QMODE+™ section in the *CARTO VISITAG™ Module User Guide.*

Before using this module:

* Read the *Instructions for Use* of the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter.
* Read the *Instructions for Use* of the TX *eco* cable.
* Read the *Instructions for Use* of the supported RF generator.

In this document, the high power, short duration mode is referred to as QMODE+™. The name in the generator may be different.

### Supported RF Generators

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_gen\_v7\_EN.xml

Consult your Biosense Webster representative regarding the availability of compatible devices in your country.

If the connected generator is not compatible with the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter, an error message appears, and the CARTO™ 3 System will not display ablation indication including the following:

* Mapping functionality is disabled
* Temperature distribution on the tip of the catheter
* Temperature indication in the Catheter View
* Ablation point tagging
* VISITAG™ Site and Grid Cell creation
* Readings Dashboard ablation mode

## TX eco Cable

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_dongle\_V7\_EN.xml

The TX *eco* Cable is used with a TX *eco* EXT Cable (extension cable) to connect a QDOT MICRO™ Catheter to the Patient Interface Unit (PIU) of the CARTO™ 3 System.

![qdot_dongle.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 161: TX eco Cable

The TX *eco* Cable transfers data from the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter to the CARTO™ 3 System and the RF generator. The information transferred is listed below.

* Force readings
* Location
* ECG signals, such as IC signals, from the 3 microelectrodes in the tip of the catheter
* Temperature measurements from the 6 thermocouples in the tip of the catheter. (Only the highest temperature is transferred to the RF generator.)

The TX *eco* Cable has two sockets: one for the TX *eco* EXT Cable, and one for catheters that connect to the MAP socket.

![qdot_dongle_socketView_annotated.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 162: Socket for TX eco EXT Cable

### TX eco Cable Warnings

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_TXWarnings\_v8\_EN.xml

* Before using the TX *eco* Cable, read the *Instructions for Use* for the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter.
* The TX *eco* Cable must be at least 1 m (39.4 inches) away from the location pad of the CARTO™ 3 System during the procedure.

REP4611 v6 tab, dongle, general, warmup

* The TX *eco* Cable must be connected to the PIU for a 5-minute warm-up period before use to ensure accurate temperature readings. After the warm-up period, the temperature readings stabilize. If the cable is used before the end of the warm-up period, a temperature drift might occur.

### Connecting the Cable

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_dongle\_connections\_V7\_EN.xml

![qdot_dongle_piu_connection.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 163: TX eco Cable Connected to the PIU

|  |  |  |  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1. | Connect the TX *eco* Cable to the CARTO™ 3 System PIU:   |  |  | | --- | --- | | 1.1 | Connect the red connector to the MAP socket on the PIU and wait until the green LED on the cable stops blinking. | | 1.2 | Connect the yellow connector to the QUAD B socket on the PIU.  You can also connect to the QUAD A or DECA socket. However, QUAD B is recommended for better signal quality on the microelectrode channels.  After the cable is connected to the PIU, allow approximately 10 seconds for the cable to initialize and for the CARTO™ 3 System to recognize it. | |
| 2. | Connect the catheter to one end of the TX *eco* EXT Cable (extension cable). |
| 3. | Connect the other end of the TX *eco* EXT Cable to the socket at the end of the TX *eco* Cable. |

### Disconnecting the Cable

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_DisconnectingTX\_v7\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Disconnect the red connector from the CARTO™ 3 System PIU. |
| 2. | Disconnect the yellow connector from the PIU. |
| 3. | After disconnecting the cable, wait 10 seconds before reconnecting the cable to the PIU. |

You can switch between a QDOT MICRO™ Catheter and a standard focal ablation catheter without disconnecting the TX *eco* Cable.

REP4611 D-V6 - 1.2.2, D-V6 - 1.2.3, REP9626\_34:1.8 V7 QMODE

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE   * If you are using a QDOT MICRO™ Catheter, both connectors must be connected to the PIU for normal functionality. Do not leave one cable connected and the other disconnected. * If you are using a standard catheter and want to use the secondary socket (for example, QUAD B), disconnect both connectors and then reconnect the red connector before using the QUAD B socket. |

### LED Status

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_dongle\_instructions\_BIT\_v7\_EN.xml

REP4611 D-V6 - 1.1.2, D-V6 - 1.1.3, D-V6 - 1.1.5, D-V6 - 1.1.13, D-V6 - 1.1.14, D-V6 - 1.1.15, D-V6 - 1.1.16, D-V6 - 1.1.17, D-V6 - 1.1.18, D-V6 - 1.1.21, D-V6- 1.3.1, D-V6- 1.3.2

After the cable is properly connected to the CARTO™ 3 System PIU and to a QDOT MICRO™ Catheter, the cable performs a Built-In Test (BIT). The status of the cable is indicated by an LED.

![qdot_dongle_annotated.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 164: LED

Table 65: LED Status

| LED | Cable Status | Troubleshooting |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Green: slow blink | BIT in progress | N/A |
| Green: solid | Ready for use | N/A |
| Red: fast blink | Error, BIT failed | The temperatures are not sent to the CARTO™ 3 System or RF generator.   1. Disconnect the catheter from the TX *eco* Cable and the TX *eco* Cable from the PIU. 2. Reconnect the TX *eco* Cable to the PIU and the catheter to the TX *eco* Cable.   If the problem persists, replace the TX *eco* Cable. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If a standard focal ablation catheter is connected, the BIT is not performed. The LED appears gray. |

To open the toolbar, right-click the map viewer background and select Toolbars > Catheter View.

| ![qdot_2D_view.png](data:image/png;base64...) | 2D display of the catheter tip   * Six small circles represent the 6 thermocouples: 3 distal and 3 proximal * Inner circle (marked by a broken line) represents the tip dome * Outer ring represents the tip sides * Four colored arcs around the circle, which correspond to the colored deflection arcs around the catheter visualization   REP4611 A-V6-1.6.3   * To view temperature (in °C), double-click the Catheter View background. The label shows the maximum temperature received from the RF generator.   During ablation, the area around each thermocouple is colored to reflect its temperature as defined in the color scale bar. |

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_dashboard\_orientation\_EN.xml

| Parallel | Perpendicular |
| --- | --- |
| ![qdot_dashboard_tipParallel.png](data:image/png;base64...) | ![qdot_dashboard_tipPerpendicular.PNG](data:image/png;base64...) |

#### 3D View of the Catheter

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_dashboard\_3D\_EN.xml

The Catheter View can also show the catheter in 3D format.

![qdot_dashboard_3D_v7p3.PNG](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 166: 3D View of QDOT MICRO™ Catheter During Ablation

In 3D format, the display of the catheter matches its visualization in the Main Map Viewer.

During mapping, the tip of the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter is green. During ablation, the temperature distribution is shown on the tip.

The catheter orientation can be synchronized or unsynchronized with the orientation in the Main Map Viewer. When it is not synchronized, you can rotate the catheter in the Catheter View independently of its orientation in the Main Map Viewer.

Click ![qmode_link_v7p3.png](data:image/png;base64...) to switch between synchronized and unsynchronized orientation.

### Mapping Options

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_mapping\_EN.xml

When a QDOT MICRO™ Catheter is used, the Micro Bipolar option is added to the map right-click menu. Select this option to view a voltage map based on the bipolar voltages of the micro-electrodes.

![map_menu_microBi.PNG](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 167: Map Types Available with QDOT MICRO™ Catheter

The highest of the three micro-electrodes bipolar peak-to-peak voltages is the one selected for each point in this type of map. In the Selected Point Viewer, the label µBi marks the channel that provided the value.

![qdot_ubi_signals.PNG](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 168: µBi Label in the Selected Point Viewer

Micro-electrodes cannot be used for electro-anatomical (EA) point acquisition.

### Displaying QDOT MICRO™ Catheter Signals

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_multielectrode\_EN.xml

The QDOT MICRO™ Catheter has 3 microelectrodes in the catheter tip. You can display IC signals from these microelectrodes in the ECG viewers by selecting the corresponding channels.

* The standard channels are labeled MAP M1 through MAP M4.
* Bipolar microelectrode channels are labeled MAP u1-u2, MAP u2-u3, and MAP u3-u1.
* Unipolar microelectrode channels are labeled MAP u1, MAP u2, and MAP u3.

![ECG_Viewers_micro.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 169: Signal Display in ECG Viewers

### Special Microelectrode Considerations

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_microelectrodes\_v6p5\_EN.xml

The locations of the microelectrodes relative to the catheter deflection colors are displayed as follows:

* µ1 is located between the red and green arcs
* µ2 is located in front of the blue arc
* µ3 is located between the red and yellow arcs

![qdot_microelectrode_locations.PNG](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 170: Location of Microelectrodes on Catheter Tip

The microelectrodes are visible on the 3D visualization of the catheter.

Recorded and Mapping Channels:

* Each microelectrode uses resources that equal the resources used by 3 electrode channels.
* The 3 bipolar microelectrode channels are selected by default in the Recorded and Mapping Channels dialog box.
* Adding or removing a microelectrode channel increases or decreases the total number of open channels by 3.

REP9626: V7 QMODE 1.9

REP4611 D-V6-1.17.1, D-V6-1.17.1

Filters:

When a QDOT MICRO™ Catheter is connected, QDOT options are added to the Filter Configuration window.

![qdot_filter_config.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 171: Filter Configuration Window with QDOT Options

QDOT options include two ECG filter settings - one for regular MAP electrodes (M1-M4: bipolar and unipolar) and one for the micro-electrodes (µElectrodes µ1-µ3: bipolar and unipolar). This allows you to set different filters on each type of electrode.

Default filters for all connectors:

* Unipolar:
  + High Pass: 2 hz
  + Low Pass: 240 hz
* Bipolar:
  + High Pass: 16 hz
  + Low Pass: 500 hz

Choosing higher High Pass filters such as 30 hz, 70 hz, 100 hz might be taken in consideration if needed.

You can save these setting to a template. Using the template will result in the following behavior:

* MAP settings will be applied to all catheters connected to the MAP socket, not just the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter.
* The micro-electrode filters will be applied and visible once the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter is connected to the CARTO™ 3 System and the Filter Configuration window is displayed.

Pacing:

REP9242\_1.2: 4.1.3 Pacing Limitation

* Pacing through the microelectrodes is limited to 6 mA.

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_catheterSetup\_EN.xml

The Catheter Setup screen shows the active sockets for a connected QDOT MICRO™ Catheter. The label MAP is added to all channels for the catheter. The microelectrodes are included in the selection of Electrodes and are labeled u1, u2 and u3.
