The post-ablation value counts up until the next ablation session can be started, and up to 4 seconds.  Usability testing requirement  ![reading_dashboard_c3v7p2.png](data:image/png;base64...) |

### Indication of Irrigation Flow Rate Changes

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/QDOT\_IrrigationIndication\_c3\_v8\_EN.xml

Indication of irrigation is automatically enabled when the generator is connected. To disable this feature, go to the Graphs tab of the Preferences dialog box, and clear the Show Irrigation Flow checkbox.

![ShowIrrigationFlow_graphs_preferences_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 173: Show Irrigation Flow Checkbox

Irrigation flow rate changes are indicated on the temperature graph (red) in the Graphs Viewer. The white dots indicate the start of a change, and the blue arrows pointing either up or down from these dots reflect the type of change in the rate of the irrigation flow (increase or decrease). This feature helps identify whether temperature changes are due to a change in the irrigation flow, or due to other reasons such as catheter movement or force change.

![GraphsViewer_ReflectionIndication_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 174: Graphs Viewer with Irrigation Indication

# Error Messages and Alerts

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ErrorMessages\_intro\_c3-v4-3\_EN.xml

REP4611 PL.21 Error 105; PL.32 Error 18, PL.33 Errors 61001,61005 and 61401; D-V1-2.5.11; D-V1-2.5.9

During a study, the following types of messages might appear:

* **Error Messages**: These messages report an error state that limits the functionality of the system. You can continue using the system, but acquisition will be disabled and other functions might not be fully operational until you correct the error.
* **Alerts**: These messages report that something in the system requires attention. Alerts may require an action, but they do not interrupt the course of the procedure.
* **Info Messages**: These messages provide information about the current status, but they do not interrupt the course of the procedure.

The System Status icon indicates whether there is a message requiring your attention. If there is, the message number is displayed near the icon.

![error_icon_v4.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 175: System Status Icon

The message display window can be displayed or hidden. If the window is hidden, click the System Status icon to open the message summary.

![Message_0002_v4.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 176: Error Message Summary

Click Details to view the full message.

The message window remains open until you close it, even if there is no error or alert message. If you close the message window, it will remain closed even if a new message appears.

See also:

* ["System Status"](#target9000087)

## System Messages

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/SystemMessages\_c3-v6\_EN.xml

In some cases, a popup system message appears (for example, when copying points to a map with a different cycle length, or if leakage current is detected). If this occurs, you must follow the instructions in the popup message before continuing.

To use the screen tips, click Help > Set Translation ScreenTips Language, and then select the language.

REP4611 A-V3-27.8.1.1

## Catheter-Related Messages

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/catheter\_messages\_C3\_v4\_EN.xml

Catheter-related messages might be generated as a result of problems with any of the following:

* Connections between catheters and the PIU
* Catheter extension cables
* Catheters

If a catheter-related message is generated, determine the source of the problem before replacing a catheter.

## Message Text

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/system\_messages\_C3\_v8p3\_EN.xml

Messages are numbered. This number is used by Biosense Webster Service and Support to identify the specific type of problem. When contacting the Biosense Webster Service and Support Department, be sure to report the message number, as well as the circumstances in which the problem was encountered.

For any problems not mentioned in this list, contact the Biosense Webster Service and Support Department.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Messages marked with an asterisk (\*) are errors if they apply to the mapping catheter; otherwise, they are alerts. |

Table 69: System-Related Messages

| Message | Action |
| --- | --- |
| Error 1  No communication with PIU detected. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that the PIU is correctly connected to the power supply and that the power supply is ON.  2. Wait one minute to ensure that the PIU has finished its power-up process.  3. Verify that the optical cable is correctly connected between the workstation and the PIU.  4. Switch the PIU Power Supply off and on again, and wait few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation. |
| Error 2  The PIU is initializing. | This process may take up to 7 minutes. |
| Error 3  The PIU is initializing. | PIU is testing the data flow integrity. This test may take one minute. |
| Error 4  The PIU Loader is not updated. | The Loader has to be updated by Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error 5  Multiple ablation generators are connected. | Disconnect one of the generators to continue with CARTO™ 3 System initialization. |
| Error 7  Leakage current detected on PIU RL input. | Perform the following to determine if the problem was caused by one of the catheters or their extension cables, by the Body Surface ECG cable, or by the PIU:  1. Immediately disconnect Body Surface ECG cable, and  all catheter extension cables from the PIU.  2. Press Reconnect in the Caution window, to enable ablation through the PIU.  3. If the error persists, shut down the PIU and contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department.  4. If the error message has disappeared, reconnect the Body Surface ECG cable, extension cables and catheters, one by one, until catheter or cable causing the leakage is identified.  5. Replace the faulty item.  6. If the error returns during ablation, disconnect all catheter cables and the Body Surface ECG cable from the PIU, turn off the PIU Power Supply, and contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department to report a PIU issue. |
| Error 8  Stimulation routing is disabled. | An error was detected in the Stimulation routing hardware of the PIU. When possible, perform the following to determine if the problem was caused by the PIU, or by one of the catheters or their extension cables:  1. Disconnect all catheters and catheter extension cables from the PIU sockets.  2. Switch the PIU Power Supply off and on again.  3. Wait a few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation.  4. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department.  5. If the problem has disappeared, reconnect each cable and catheter, one by one, and check if the error re-appears after a particular cable or catheter connection. |
| Error 9  PIU Channel calibration error. | Accuracy of the CARTO™ 3 System may be compromised when this error appears.  Perform the following:  1. Switch off the Power Supply to the PIU, disconnect the cables connected to the REF/DECA, 20 POLE A and 20 POLE B sockets of the PIU, and switch the Power Supply on again.  2. Wait a few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation.  3. If the error disappeared, reconnect the cables to the PIU and continue.  4. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error 10  PIU initialization failure. | Perform the following:  1. Switch the Power Supply to the PIU off and on again.  2. Wait a few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation.  3. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error 13  ECG cards are not of same version. | ECG cards of same version must be installed by Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error 14  PIU firmware has to be reloaded. Restart the PIU. | The loaded PIU firmware version is not compatible with the ACL Tx card. Switch the PIU power supply off and on again. Upon restart, the compatible PIU firmware version will be automatically loaded. |
| Error 17  Location Pad currents unstable. | Perform the following:  1. Switch off the PIU power supply.  2. Disconnect and re-connect the Location Pad to the PIU.  3. Disconnect and re-connect the Location Pad extension cable and switch on the power supply.  4. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Alert 18  The PIU Temperature is too high. | Perform the following:  1. Ensure that the PIU is properly ventilated.  2. Switch off the PIU power and let the PIU cool down, for a few minutes.  3. Switch on the power supply. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error 19  Failure in PIU ECG1 card BIT. | Perform the following:  1. Switch the Power Supply to the PIU off and on again.  2. Wait a few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation.  3. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error 20  Failure in PIU ECG2 card BIT. | Perform the following:  1. Switch the Power Supply to the PIU off and on again.  2. Wait a few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation.  3. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error 21  Failure in PIU ECG3 card BIT. | Perform the following:  1. Switch the Power Supply to the PIU off and on again.  2. Wait a few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation.  3. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Alert 23  Failure in PIU ACL Tx card BIT. | This may affect catheter visualization or proximity indication.  Perform the following:  1. Switch the Power Supply to the PIU off and on again.  2. Wait a few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation.  3. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error 24  Failure in PIU Mag Tx card BIT. | Perform the following:  1. Switch the Power Supply to the PIU off and on again.  2. Wait a few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation.  3. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error 25  Failure in PIU Loc Rx card BIT. | Perform the following to determine if the problem was caused by the PIU, or by one of the catheters or their extension cables:  1. Disconnect all catheters and catheter extension cables from the PIU sockets.  2. Switch the PIU Power Supply off and on again.  3. Wait a few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation.  4. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department.  5. If the problem has disappeared, reconnect each cable and catheter, one by one, and check if the errors reappears after a particular cable or catheter connection. |
| Error 27  Failure in PIU Power card BIT. | Perform the following:  1. Switch the Power Supply to the PIU off and on again.  2. Wait a few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation.  3. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error 29  A magnetic data error occurred. | Perform the following:  1. Switch the Power Supply to the PIU off and on again.  2. Restart the CARTO™ 3 Application.  3. Wait a few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation.  4. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 40  MAP: defective catheter or cable. | Voltage supplied by the Loc Rx card to the MAP socket exceeds the tolerance settings. This may be caused by a defective catheter or its extension cable, or by a defective Loc Rx card in the PIU.  Perform the following to determine if the problem is caused by the PIU, or the catheter or the extension cable:  1. Disconnect the extension cable from the MAP socket. If the error message disappears, the catheter or its extension cable is defective. Verify that there is no moisture in the connectors of the catheter and the extension cable. Replace the faulty catheter or cable.  2. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department to report a PIU issue. |
| Error\* 41  REF/DECA: defective catheter or cable. | Voltage supplied by the Loc Rx card to the REF/DECA socket exceeds the tolerance settings. This may be caused by a defective catheter or its extension cable, or by a defective Loc Rx card in the PIU.  Perform the following to determine if the problem is caused by the PIU, or the catheter or the extension cable:  1. Disconnect the extension cable from the REF/DECA socket. If the error message disappears, the catheter or its extension cable is defective. Verify that there is no moisture in the connectors of the catheter and the extension cable. Replace the faulty catheter or cable.  2. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department to report a PIU issue. |
| Error\* 42  20 POLE A: defective catheter or cable. | Voltage supplied by the Loc Rx card to the 20 POLE A socket exceeds the tolerance settings. This may be caused by a defective catheter or its cable, or by a defective Loc Rx card in the PIU.  Perform the following to determine if the problem is caused by the PIU, or the catheter or the extension cable:  1. Disconnect the extension cable from the 20 POLE A socket. If the error message has disappeared, the catheter or its extension cable is defective. Verify that there is no moisture in the connectors of the catheter and the extension cable. Replace the faulty catheter or cable.  2. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department to report a PIU issue. |
| Error\* 43  20 POLE B: defective catheter or cable. | The voltage supplied by the Rx card to the 20 POLE B socket is out of tolerance. This may be caused by a defective catheter or its cable or by a defective Rx card in the PIU.  Perform the following to determine if the problem is caused by the PIU, or the catheter or the extension cable:  1. Disconnect the extension cable from the 20 POLE B socket. If the error disappeared, the catheter or the extension cable is defective. Verify that there is no moisture in the connectors of the catheter and the extension cable. Replace the catheter or the cable.  2. If the error persists, the problem is in the PIU. Contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error 44  ULTRASOUND: defective catheter or cable. | Voltage supplied by the Loc Rx card to the ULTRASOUND socket exceeds the tolerance settings. This may be caused by a defective catheter or its cable, or by a defective Loc Rx card in the PIU.  Perform the following to determine if the problem is caused by the PIU, or the catheter or the extension cable:  1. Disconnect the extension cable from the ULTRASOUND socket. If the error message has disappeared, the catheter or its extension cable is defective. Verify that there is no moisture in the connectors of the catheter and the extension cable. Replace the faulty catheter or cable.  2. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department to report a PIU issue. |
| Alert 79  Force reading accuracy might be reduced. | The force catheter indicates a force reading with reduced accuracy.  To ensure accurate force readings, perform the following:  1. Verify that there is no other catheter or other metallic object in close proximity to the force catheter tip.  2. If the force reading is not near zero when the catheter is not in contact with tissue, perform zeroing. |
| Alert 80  Invalid force measurements. | Ensure that all four tip electrodes are outside the sheath. |
| Alert 81  Force catheter warm-up. | Temperature cannot be read from the force catheter. While the force catheter in its 15-minute warm-up phase, force measurement accuracy might be reduced.  After 15 minutes from catheter connection, perform zeroing again. |
| Alert 82  Force measurements might be inaccurate. | Force integrity is not calculated and therefore the force measurements received from the force catheter might be inaccurate.  If the problem persists, switch the PIU Power Supply off and on again. Wait until communication between the PIU and the workstation is established. |
| Alert 83  Invalid force measurements. | Force measurements received from the force catheter are invalid. It is possible to continue using the catheter; however, force measurement is disabled.  To use force data, perform the following until resolved:  1. Disconnect and reconnect the catheter.  2. Restart the application.  3. Switch the PIU power supply off and on again. Wait until communication between the PIU and the workstation is established. |
| Alert 84  Force reading accuracy is reduced. | The force catheter indicates a force reading with reduced accuracy.  The catheter is too close to the Location Pad. If necessary, continue the study without force data. |
| Alert 85  Force is not displayed due to high interference. | High interference is detected to the force integrity. Force data cannot be displayed.  The interference may be caused by another catheter or other metal object in a close proximity to the force catheter tip.  To receive force readings, move the interfering catheter farther away from the force catheter tip.  If no catheter is found near the force catheter tip, verify that the force catheter is not in contact with the tissue and perform zeroing. |
| Alert 86  Force catheter requires zeroing. | To ensure accurate force readings, verify that the force reading is near zero when the catheter is not in contact with tissue. If the force reading is not near zero when the catheter is not in contact with tissue, perform zeroing. |
| Alert 87  Force catheter may require zeroing. | To ensure accurate force readings, verify that the force reading is near zero when the catheter is not in contact with tissue. If the force reading is not near zero when the catheter is not in contact with tissue, perform zeroing. |
| Alert 88  Force reading accuracy might be reduced. | Interference is detected to the force integrity.  The interference may be caused by another catheter or other metal object in a close proximity to the force catheter tip.  To ensure accurate force readings, move the interfering catheter farther away from the force catheter tip.  If no catheter is found near the force catheter tip, verify that the force catheter is not in contact with the tissue and perform zeroing. |
| Alert 89  Force reading accuracy is reduced. | The force catheter indicates a force reading with reduced accuracy, due to high lateral force applied to the catheter tip. |
| Alert 90  MAP: test catheter detected. | The catheter connected to the MAP socket is detected as a test catheter. It is not for human use!  This catheter is intended for system testing by qualified service personnel. While the testing catheter is connected, maps are limited to 30 points. |

| Error 228  Eco cable not connected to QUAD or DECA socket. | The eco cable is partially connected. | Alert 229  Eco cable not connected to MAP socket. | The eco cable is partially connected. In order to enable the eco cable functionality connect both of the eco cable’s connectors to the PIU. | Error 255  Unstable Body Reference location. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Ensure that the Back Patches' sensors are in place.  2. Ensure that the patient is at rest.  3. Ensure that neither Fluoroscope nor Table is moving.  This message will close when the problem is resolved. |
| Error 256  Patient Body Reference has moved. | The patient Body Reference (Back Patches) has changed position and cannot be compensated by the system. No new data can be added to existing maps unless the error is resolved.  Perform the following until resolved:  1. If the error occurred after a change in the Fluoroscope or patient table position, return the Fluoroscope or patient table to its previous position.  2. Verify that the patient is relaxed and lying flat.  3. If not resolved, click "Learn new Body Reference". Previous maps will no longer be valid for addition of new data unless "Continue" is clicked to accept the expected Map Shift. |
| Alert 257  Back Patch detached. | One or more of the Back Patches has partially detached or has insufficient contact with the skin.  Perform the following:  1. Open the Location Setup.  2. Identify the detached Patch.  3. Press the Patch to the skin, without moving it. |

If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |

Wiring to all patches is well connected. |

| Alert 318  MAP: catheter in reduced accuracy zone. | The catheter connected to the MAP socket is within the reduced accuracy zone.  If the catheter cannot be moved into the accuracy zone, be aware that accuracy will be reduced. |
| Error\* 319  MAP: catheter out of mapping range. | The catheter connected to the MAP socket appears to be out of the mapping range.  If the catheter is placed within the mapping zone but the error persists, this may be due to severe metal interference, or due to a defective catheter or its extension cable. | Error 370  Mapping catheter not connected. | No catheter is recognized at the socket selected for mapping. Perform the following until the Acquire button is enabled:  1. Verify that a mapping catheter is connected to the socket selected for mapping.  2. Change the selection of the mapping catheter socket.  3. Disconnect and reconnect the catheter and ensure good connection.  4. Replace the cable or the catheter. |
| Error 371  Cannot acquire points with mapping catheter. | None of the electrodes of the selected mapping catheter are available for mapping. Perform the following until the Acquire button is enabled:  1. Ensure that catheter visualization initialization has finished and that electrodes selected for mapping are out of sheath.  2. Ensure that the selected mapping catheter is visualized. If not, check that the Calibration Matrix is sufficient in the region of the catheter.  3. Disconnect and reconnect the catheter and ensure good connection.  4. Replace the cable or the catheter. |
| Error 372  Cannot acquire points with a non-mapping catheter. | A non-mapping catheter is connected to the socket selected for mapping. Points cannot be acquired using that catheter. Perform the following until Acquiring is enabled:  1. Replace the catheter with a mapping catheter.  2. Change the selection of the mapping catheter socket. |
| Alert 373  Reference catheter is not connected. | The catheter selected for Reference Channels is disconnected. Connect the catheter to enable Reference Annotation calculation. |
| Alert 374  No ventricular Reference Annotations are detected. | Body Surface signals are low quality. Perform the following:  1. Verify good connection of ECG electrodes to the patient.  2. Verify proper connection of the ECG cable.  3. Inspect the precordial lead signals and verify that there is no noise. |
| Alert 375  No atrial Reference Annotations are detected. | The intracardiac signals are low quality. Perform the following:  1. Verify that the reference catheter is properly connected.  2. Verify that the bipolar reference signals are of good quality and include atrial activity. |
| Alert 376  Atrial Reference Annotation might be compromised. | Atrial Reference Annotation might be compromised due to low quality of Body Surface signals. Perform the following:  1. Verify good connection of ECG electrodes to the patient.  2. Verify proper connection of the ECG cable.  3. Inspect the precordial lead signals and verify that there is no noise. |
| Alert 377  No atrial Reference Annotations are detected. | Atrial activations are concurrent with ventricular activations. Perform the following:  1. Try repositioning the reference catheter in the chamber to improve the atrial signal timing.  2. Consider using a Body Surface Reference Channel, if applicable.  3. Use a different Reference Criterion. |
| Alert 379  Reference Annotation Algorithm is initializing. | The Reference Annotation calculation is in progress. Wait few seconds until calculation is complete. |
| Alert 382  Reference Annotations are momentarily unavailable | The Reference Annotation Algorithm is adjusting the detection threshold. Wait a few seconds until annotations are detected again. |
| Error 399  Ref. & Map annotations selected from same catheter. | Map points cannot be built. Select Reference and Mapping annotations from different catheters. |
| Alert 400  No Reference Annotations are detected. | Verify that the Reference Annotations are detected consistently in the Annotation Viewer. |
| Error 401  Map points cannot be acquired. | Perform the following until point Acquisition is enabled and the issue is resolved:  1. In the Map Setup, verify that Reference Annotation and Mapping Annotation are detected consistently.  2. Verify that the Patch unit and the mapping catheter are connected properly and detected by the system.  3. Verify that there is no severe distortion error related to the mapping catheter.  4. Verify that at least one mapping channel of the selected type (bipolar or unipolar) is recorded.  5. Switch the power supply to the PIU off and on again. Wait a few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation. |
| Alert 402  MAP: magnetic distortion. | A distortion in the magnetic field has been detected near the catheter connected to the MAP socket. The location data received from this catheter may be inaccurate.  Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that metal objects have not been introduced into the field.  2. Verify that the Fluoroscopy Detector is not too close to patient's chest. If it is, raise its position.  3. Verify that the Fluoroscope tube is not too close to the Location Pad. If it is, raise the table, or change the Fluoroscope position so that the tube is farther away from the Location Pad.  4. Verify that the handle of the catheter or the eco cable, connected to the MAP socket, are at least 30 cm from the Location Pad. |
| Error\* 410  MAP: severe magnetic distortion. | A severe distortion in the magnetic field has been detected near the catheter connected to the MAP socket. The location data received from this catheter are not accurate.  Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that metal objects have not been introduced into the field.  2. Verify that the Fluoroscopy Detector is not too close to patient's chest. If it is, raise its position.  3. Verify that the Fluoroscope tube is not too close to the Location Pad. If it is, raise the table, or change the Fluoroscope position so that the tube is farther away from the Location Pad.  4. Verify that the handle of the catheter or the eco cable, connected to the MAP socket, are at least 30 cm from the Location Pad.  5. Replace the cable of the catheter.  6. Replace the catheter. |
| Alert 411  REF/DECA: magnetic distortion. | A distortion in the magnetic field has been detected near the catheter connected to the REF/DECA socket. The location data received from this catheter may be inaccurate.  Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that metal objects have not been introduced into the field.  2. Verify that the Fluoroscopy Detector is not too close to patient's chest. If it is, raise its position.  3. Verify that the Fluoroscope tube is not too close to the Location Pad. If it is, raise the table, or change the Fluoroscope position so that the tube is farther away from the Location Pad.  4. Verify that the handle of the catheter and the eco adapter are at least 30 cm from the Location Pad. |
| Alert 412  Patch 1: magnetic distortion. | A distortion in the magnetic field has been detected near Back Patch 1. Mapping is not recommended under these conditions. Initialization will not finish while distortion exists.  Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that metal objects have not been introduced into the field.  2. Verify that the Fluoroscope tube is not too close to the Location Pad. If it is, raise the table or change the Fluoroscope position so that the tube is farther away from the Location Pad.  3. Verify that the Patch unit is at least 50 cm from the Location Pad.  If the problem persists, replace the Patch Unit. |
| Error 413  Patch 1: severe magnetic distortion. | A severe distortion in the magnetic field has been detected near Back Patch 1. Mapping is not possible under these conditions.  Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that metal objects have not been introduced into the field.  2. Verify that the Fluoroscope tube is not too close to the Location Pad. If it is, raise the table or change the Fluoroscope position so that the tube is farther away from the Location Pad.  3. Verify that the Patch unit is at least 50 cm from the Location Pad.  If the problem persists, replace the Patch Unit. |
| Alert 414  Patch 4: magnetic distortion. | A distortion in the magnetic field has been detected near Chest Patch 4. Mapping is not recommended under these conditions. Initialization cannot be finished while distortion exists.  Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that metal objects have not been introduced into the field.  2. Verify that the Chest Patches are within the Mapping Zone.  3. Verify that the Fluoroscopy Detector is not too close to patient's chest. If it is, raise its position.  4. | Error\* 980  SPU: Connected to 20 POLE B in PIU instead of 20 POLE A. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that the extension cable is securely connected to the 20 POLE A socket on the PIU. Confirm the connection in the Catheter Setup screen.  2. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 981  SPU: Connected to 20 POLE A in PIU instead of 20 POLE B. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that the extension cable is securely connected to the 20 POLE B socket on the PIU. Confirm the connection in the Catheter Setup screen.  2. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 982  SPU: Synchronization error. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that the extension cables are securely connected to the 20 POLE A and 20 POLE B sockets. Confirm the connection in the Catheter Setup screen.  2. If pacing is active through the catheter connected to the SPU, stop pacing.  3. Wait 30 seconds. If the error remains, disconnect the catheter and reboot the SPU.  4. If the error persists, switch the PIU Power Supply off and on again. Please note that power reset is not recommended during procedure  5. Replace the extension cable between the 20 POLE A socket on the PIU and the To Pole A socket on the SPU.  6. Replace the extension cable between the 20 POLE B socket on the PIU and the To Pole B socket on the SPU.  7. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 983  SPU: ECG self-test failure. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Disconnect the catheter and reboot the SPU.  2. If the error persists, switch the PIU Power Supply off and on again. Please note that power reset is not recommended during procedure.  3. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 984  SPU: Version mismatch. | Disconnect the catheter and reboot the SPU.  If the problem re-occurs after SPU power reboot, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 985  SPU: Communication error. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that the SPU’s front LED is lit. If not, connect the Power Supply cable to the SPU.  2. Verify that the fiber-optic cable is securely connected to the PIU and the SPU.  3. Wait until the LED indicates that the SPU is ready.  4. If the error persists, switch the PIU Power Supply off and on again. Please note that power reset is not recommended during a procedure  5. Replace the fiber-optic cable.  6. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Alert 986  SPU: Temperature is high. | Verify that the SPU vent openings are not covered. |
| Error\* 987  SPU: Temperature reading is out of range. | DO NOT TOUCH SPU! MAY CAUSE BURN.  If the problem persists after SPU power reboot, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 988  SPU: 20 POLE B is not connected. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that the extension cable is securely connected to the 20 POLE B socket on the PIU. Confirm the connection in the Catheter Setup screen.  2. Replace the extension cable between the 20 POLE B socket on the PIU and the To Pole B socket on the SPU.  3. Disconnect the catheter and reboot the SPU.  4. If the error persists, switch the PIU Power Supply off and on. Please note that power reset is not recommended during a procedure.  5. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 989  SPU: Unsupported pacing channels. | Stop pacing.  Perform the following until resolved:  1.If there is pacing through the Recording system, define pacing channels as described in the Signal Processing Unit Instructions for Use and Release Notes.  2. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 991  SPU: Connected to REF/DECA in PIU instead of 20 POLE A. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that the extension cable is securely connected to the 20 POLE A socket on the PIU. Confirm the connection in the Catheter Setup screen.  2. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 992  SPU: Connected to REF/DECA in PIU instead of 20 POLE B. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 993  SPU: Power card sensor reading is out of range. | Disconnect the catheter and reboot the SPU.  If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 994  SPU: Initialization not complete. | Disconnect the catheter and reboot the SPU.  If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 995  SPU: 20 POLE A is not connected. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that the extension cable is securely connected to the 20 POLE A socket on the PIU. Disconnect the catheter and reboot the SPU.  4. If the error persists, switch the PIU Power Supply off and on. Please note that power reset is not recommended during a procedure.  5. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Alert 996  SPU: Synchronization warning. | Verify that the extension cables are securely connected to the 20 POLE A and 20 POLE B sockets. Confirm the connection in the Catheter Setup screen.  If the problem persists for more than 10 minutes, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 997  SPU: Software installation incomplete. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that the SPU’s front LED is lit. If not, connect the Power Supply cable to the SPU.  2. Verify that the fiber-optic cable is securely connected to the PIU and the SPU.  3. Wait until the LED indicates that the SPU is ready. If the error persists, disconnect the catheter and reboot the SPU.  4. If the error persists, switch the PIU Power Supply off and on again. Please note that power reset is not recommended during a procedure.  5. Restart the CARTO™ 3 Application.  6. Replace the fiber-optic cable.  7. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 998  SPU: ECG data streaming error. | Disconnect the catheter and reboot the SPU.  If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 999  SPU: Self-test in progress. | Disconnect the catheter extender from the SPU.  Keep the catheter disconnected until the LED changes from blue to steady green. |
| Error 1003  The Patch Unit appears disconnected. | Verify that the Patch Unit is properly connected. This message will close as the Patch Unit is detected. |
| Error 1006  Back Patch sensor error. | The magnetic sensor of one of the Back Patches is defective. Replace the Back Patches cable, or the whole Patch unit. |
| Alert 1007  Chest Patch sensor error. | The magnetic sensor of one of the Chest Patches is defective. Replace the Chest Patches cable, or the whole Patch unit.  Note that this issue may affect ACL catheter visualization and ACCURESP™ Module data. |
| Alert 1008  Back Patch disconnected or fully detached. | One of the Back Patches is disconnected or fully detached from the patient.  Identify the disconnected patch in the Location Setup, and then perform the following until resolved:  1. Reconnect the wire to the patch.  2. Reattach the patch to the patient. |
| Alert 1009  Chest Patch disconnected or fully detached. | One of the Chest Patches is disconnected or fully detached from the patient.  Identify the disconnected patch in the Location Setup, and then perform the following until resolved:  1. Reconnect the wire to the patch.  2. Reattach the patch to the patient. |

| Alert 1012  Chest Patches cable not detected. | Verify that the cable for the Chest Patches is properly connected to the Patch Unit. If the problem persists, replace the cable. |
| Alert 1049  REF/DECA: catheter in reduced accuracy zone. | The catheter connected to the REF/DECA socket is within a reduced accuracy zone.  If there is no option to move the catheter into the accuracy zone, be aware that accuracy will be reduced. |
| Error\* 1050  REF/DECA: catheter out of mapping range. | The catheter connected to the REF/DECA socket appears to be out of the mapping range.  If the catheter is placed within the mapping zone but the error persists, this may be due to a severe metal interference, or due to a defective catheter or its extension cable. Perform the following until resolved:  1. Make sure that there is no severe metal interference to the catheter or to the Location Pad, from the Fluoroscope or other metal objects.  2. Replace the catheter extension cable.  3. Replace the catheter. |
| Error 1052  Back Patches are out of mapping range. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Ensure that the Back Patches' sensors are in place.  2. Open the Location Setup screen and move the patches into the mapping range, by adjusting the Location Pad or the patches.  3. Verify that the Fluoroscopy Detector is not too close to patient's chest. If it is, raise its position  4. Verify that the Fluoroscope tube is not too close to the Location Pad. If it is, raise the table or change the Fluoroscope position so that the tube is farther away from the Location Pad.  5. Replace the Patch Unit.  If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Alert 1053  Chest Patches are out of mapping range. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Ensure that the Chest Patches' sensors are in place.  2. Open the Location Setup screen and move the patches into the mapping range, by adjusting the Location Pad or the patches.  3. Verify that the Fluoroscopy Detector is not too close to patient's chest. If it is, raise its position.  4. Verify that the Fluoroscope tube is not too close to the Location Pad. If it is, raise the table or change the Fluoroscope position so that the tube is farther away from the Location Pad.  5. Replace the Patch Unit.  If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Alert 1054  Chest Patch: partially detached or bad contact. | One or more of the Chest Patches has partially detached or has bad contact with the skin.  Perform the following:  1. Open the Location Setup.  2. Identify the detached Patch.  3. Press the Patch to the skin, without moving it. |
| Alert 1101  The hard disk is almost full. | When possible, exit the study and create more space by backing up patient data, and then deleting it from the hard disk drive. |
| Alert 1102  The hard disk is full. | When possible, exit the study and create more space by backing up patient data, and then deleting it from the hard disk drive. |
| Alert 1103  Memory has almost no free space. | Avoid acquiring more data to the study. System performance might be degraded. When possible, restart the CARTO™ 3 Application. |
| Alert 1104  There is not enough free memory. | Reboot the workstation. |
| Error 1108  Reference conflict. | Current Body Reference (Back Patches) location is different from the active map's Reference location. The Acquire button is disabled.  Points cannot be acquired to the active map. Open a new map, or select another map. |
| Alert 1201  ECG limb lead disconnection. | An ECG limb lead has a bad connection or is totally disconnected. Please check the four ECG limb connections. |
| Info 1210  Acquisition disabled. | Real-time point and FAM acquisition cannot be performed in Timeline mode. Switch to real-time mode to continue. |
| Info 1220  Continuous acquisition disabled. | When the CFAE Module is in use continuous acquisition is disabled. To enable continuous acquisition, disable the CFAE Module. |
| Alert 1221  Continuous acquisition disabled. | Continuous acquisition is disabled due to one of the following:  1. The number of points in the active map reached the limit for continuous acquisition.  2. The number of points in the study reached the limit for continuous acquisition.  To continue continuous point acquisition, either switch to another active map or start a new study. |
| Alert 1222  Continuous acquisition disabled. | Continuous acquisition is disabled due to an error related to the force catheter or the CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module. |
| Alert 1402  No communication with Fluoroscopy system. | Verify that the communication cable between the CARTO™ 3 System and to the Fluoroscopy system is properly connected. |
| Alert 1403  Registration to Fluoroscopy system not completed. | Complete the registration process before continuing. |
| Alert 1404  Unsupported table or Fluoroscopy position. | The registration is invalid due to unsupported position of the Fluoroscopy system or the patient table. Importing new Fluoroscopy captures is disabled and location data may be inaccurate. Move the Fluoroscopy system or the patient table to the last used position. |
| Alert 1406  No communication with Fluoroscopy system. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Check that the DICOM network cable is properly connected between the CARTO™ 3 System workstation and the DICOM network.  2. Check that the DICOM network cable is properly connected between the Fluoroscopy system and the DICOM network.  3. Verify that the DICOM configuration parameters are correct in both the CARTO™ 3 System and the Fluoroscopy system. |
| Alert 1500  Respiration gating training is required. | Start respiration gating training. |
| Alert 2600  Simultaneous ablation and pacing not allowed. | To enable ablation through these electrodes, deselect Stimulation Routing from them. |
| Alert 2601  End-expirium data missing. | The respiration-related data, required for proper operation of the CARTO VISITAG™ Module, is not available.  Perform the following until resolved:  1. Perform Respiration training using ACCURESP™ Module.  2. Disable the Position Respiration Adjustment option in CARTO VISITAG™ Module preferences. |
| Alert 2604  Force values missing. | The force data required for one or more of the current VISITAG™ Preferences is not available.  Perform the following until resolved:  1. Ensure that CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module license is active.  2. Ensure that a force catheter is connected and is used as the ablation catheter.  3. Ensure that the function is working properly and force graphs show valid data.  4. Disable the Force Over Time filter in CARTO VISITAG™ Module preferences. |
| Alert 2606  Stability+ training is active. | VISITAG™ Sites will not be created during this period due to unknown catheter stability.  Stability+ training may take up to 2 minutes. |
| Alert 2607  Stability+ data is invalid. | VISITAG™ Sites will not be created during this period due to unknown catheter stability.  The data required to use Stability+ is not valid.  Perform the following until resolved:  1. Confirm that the ablation catheter is in the mapping zone and that it is connected to the PIU or extension cable.  2. Verify that the patches are properly connected and positioned, and that they do not overlap on the patient's body, including ECG and ablation patches.  3. Verify that no metal objects have been introduced into the field.  Stability+ reset will start when the issue is resolved. |
| Alert 2608  Stability+ reset is active. | VISITAG™ Sites will not be created during this period due to unknown catheter stability.  Stability+ reset may take up to 20 seconds. |
| Error 2610  The ablation data received by the CARTO 3 system is not from the currently selected generator. |  |
| Error 6150  Ultrasound catheter sensor error. | The magnetic sensor of the catheter is disconnected. To continue, replace the catheter cable. If that does not resolve the problem, replace the catheter. |
| Alert 6951  Ultrasound catheter is in reduced accuracy zone. | If there is no option to move the catheter into the accuracy zone, be aware that accuracy will be reduced. |

Table 70: Ultrasound-Related Messages

| Message | Action |
| --- | --- |
| Error 600  An error occurred in the CARTO™ 3 System. Recording of ultrasound clips is currently disabled. | Check the error message and resolve it according to the instructions provided. |
| Error 6100  An ultrasound catheter error occurred. | An EEPROM Checksum error occurred. Replace the catheter to continue. |
| Error 6101  An ultrasound catheter error occurred. | An invalid catheter ID is detected. Replace the catheter to continue. |
| Error 6102  An ultrasound catheter error occurred. | The catheter was not properly calibrated. Replace the catheter to continue. |
| Error 6103  Time allowed for use of the ultrasound catheter has expired. | Replace the catheter. |
| Error 6105  An ultrasound catheter error occurred. | An EEPROM Checksum error occurred. Replace the cable to continue. |
| Error 6106  An ultrasound catheter Cable error occurred. | The cable was not properly calibrated. Replace the cable to continue. |
| Error 6107  An ultrasound catheter error occurred. | Invalid re-processing data is detected. Replace the catheter to continue. |
| Error 6108  An error related to the ultrasound catheter occurred. | The catheter cable is not detected.  Reconnect or replace the cable to continue. |
| Error 6109  An ultrasound catheter Cable error occurred. | An invalid cable ID is detected. Replace the cable to continue. |
| Error 6150  Ultrasound catheter sensor error. | The magnetic sensor of the catheter is disconnected. To continue, replace the catheter cable. If that does not resolve the problem, replace the catheter. |
| Error 6303  No ultrasound catheter is connected. | Connect an ultrasound catheter to enable contour acquisition. |
| Error 6402  A severe distortion in the magnetic field has been detected near the ultrasound catheter. | The distortion is above the recommended threshold. Mapping is not possible under these conditions.  Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that metal objects have not been introduced into the field.  2. Verify that the Fluoroscopy Detector is not too close to patient chest. If it is, raise its position.  3. Verify that the catheter's handle is at least 30 cm from the Location Pad.  4. Replace the cable of the catheter.  5. Replace the catheter. |
| Error 6900  The ultrasound catheter appears disconnected. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Connect an ultrasound catheter.  2. Check all related cabling connections and reconnect, if necessary.  3. Replace the cable or the catheter, if the problem persists. |
| Error 61001  No Ethernet communication with ultrasound system is detected. | Verify that the communication cable to the ultrasound system is properly connected and that the communication is properly set on both systems. |
| Error 61004  Communication initialization with the ultrasound system is in process. | Wait while the communication with the ultrasound system is initializing. |
| Error 61120  Unsupported ultrasound mode or layout. The ultrasound images might not be live images. | Return the ultrasound machine to a supported mode and layout. |
| Error 61121  No live image from the ultrasound machine. Freeze mode is active. | Unfreeze to continue. |
| Error 61122  Unsupported ultrasound system mode - Zoom mode is active. | Return to 2D mode to continue. |
| Error 61220  The ultrasound machine is in error state. |  |
| Error 61301  The system was unable to create an ultrasound clip. Please try again. |  |
| Error 61401  No video signal or unstable video from the ultrasound system is detected. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. (If video over LAN is not used) Verify that the video cable is properly connected.  2. Restart the ultrasound module in CARTO™ 3 System, by de-selecting and reselecting the ultrasound system in the Study Setup screen. |
| Error 61402  Inconsistency in streaming from the ultrasound system is detected | Clear and reselect the ultrasound system in the Study Setup Screen |
| Error 61403  Unsupported probe is selected. | Select a supported ICE probe in the ULS System to continue |
| Alert 61505  Ultrasound data acquisition is not possible as one of the following limits was reached:  • Contours or frames per clip  • Contours, frames, or clips per map  • Contours, frames, or clips per study  • Clips per study | Unassign or permanently delete contours or frames, that are no longer needed to continue acquiring additional ultrasound data. |
| Alert 61509  Unrecommended Chamber of Interest. | The point list contains input frames from maps with the chamber of interest other than LA. CARTOSOUND™ FAM Module is only supported for use in the left atrium |

# Menus, Shortcuts, and Related Icons

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Menus\_Shortcuts\_Icons\_EN.xml

This chapter provides an overview of all menu options and how they are accessed.

All options marked with an asterisk (\*) are available in Review Mode only. Options marked with a double asterisk (\*\*) are available in Acquisition Mode only.

## Main Menu Options

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Main\_menu\_options\_v3-2\_EN.xml

The main menu options are located at the upper left of the main screen. The available options may vary, depending on the installed software modules.

![menu_mainOptions_v3-2.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 179: Main Menu Options

### Study Menu

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/study\_menu\_6\_EN.xml

Table 71: Study Menu Options

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Continue Study\* | Opens the current study in Acquisition mode.  Note that the location reference must be in its original position to be able to continue the study.  See ["Continuing a Study from Review Mode"](#target9000090). |
| Rename Study | Opens the Rename Study dialog box for giving a new name to the current study. |
| Edit Patient Details | Opens the Edit Patient Details dialog box for entering or changing the patient data. |
| Load Maps From Previous Study | Loads maps from a previous study while the CARTO™ 3 System is in acquisition mode.  See ["Loading Maps from a Previous Study"](#target9000091). |
| Print | Opens a dialog box with printing options.  See ["Changing the Background Color"](#target9000092) for optimal settings. |
| Export Study Data\* | Exports the ECG data in an ASCII file that can be used in a database program such as Microsoft Excel.  After exiting the study, the exported data can be accessed through the Export Data dialog box.  See ["Exporting Study Data as Text Files"](#target9000093). |
| Save Template As | Opens the Save Template dialog box for saving user-defined parameters to a different template.  See ["Creating a Template"](#target9000094). |
| Exit | Closes the CARTO™ 3 System application and returns to the opening screen.  Before exiting, you can back up the Study.  See ["Exiting the CARTO™ 3 System"](#target9000095). |

### Map Menu

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/map\_menu\_v8p3\_EN.xml

Table 72: Map Menu Options

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| New Map \*\* | Starts a new map.  See ["Map Setup Screen"](#target9000096). |
| Remap \*\* | Creates a structure-only copy of the active map. |
| Map Replay | Recalculates the map based on different acquisition filters.  Refer to the *CARTO PRIME™ Module User Guide*. |
| Save Map As | Opens the Save As dialog box for saving a copy of the active map. The copy must have a different name. |
| Adjust FAM Point-by-Point | Combines electroanatomical points with the FAM reconstruction, updating the reconstruction at the position nearest to the point.  See ["Combining EA Points with the FAM Reconstruction"](#target9000097). |
| Fill & Color Threshold | Opens the Fill and Color Threshold dialog box.  The Fill Threshold specifies the extent to which the reconstruction is displayed around each point.  The Color Threshold specifies the extent to which color interpolation is applied around each point.  See ["Adjusting the Color Threshold"](#target9000098). |
| Anatomical Structure | Opens the Anatomical Structure dialog box.  Select a region of the map to enable the options in the dialog box. After the structure is defined, hide or show it from the Display menu.  See ["Anatomical Structures"](#target9000099). |
| Design Line | Opens the Design Line dialog box for applying a Design Line to the active map. |
| Distance Measurement - Standard | Measures the actual distance in space between any two points on a map, on two maps, or on a map and a surface image.  Not available in the Additional Map Viewer.  See ["Distance Measurement Tool"](#target9000100). |
| Distance Measurement - Catheter | Measures the distance between the real-time catheter tip position and the point, point tag, or VISITAG Location that was created. Maximum distance is 15 mm.  Not available in the Additional Map Viewer.  See ["Distance Measurement Tool"](#target9000101). |
| Area Measurement | Opens the Area Measurement tool for measuring an area of the map.  See ["Area Measurement Tool"](#target9000102). |
| Propagation | Displays a propagation map.  Available only in maps with electroanatomical points.  See ["Viewing a Propagation Map"](#target9000103). |
| Ripple Map | Opens the Ripple Viewer.  Refer to the *CONFIDENSE™ Module User Guide*. |
| LAT Histogram | Opens the LAT Histogram window.  See ["LAT Histogram Window"](#target9000104). |
| Playback\* | Opens the Playback tools for reviewing acquisition of the map, point by point. |
| Early Meets Late | Opens the Early Meets Late dialog box. Points that meet the specified criterion are colored a dark red.  See ["Early Meets Late Option"](#target9000105). |
| Surface Reconstruction | Displays the map on a single 3D surface that intersects with all acquired points. |
| LAO/RAO Orientation | Opens a submenu for selecting the orientation. Options are:   * RAO 45, LAO 45 * RAO 30, LAO 60   See ["Defining the LAO and RAO Orientation"](#target9000106). |
| Pattern Bank | Opens the Pattern Bank dialog box for reviewing the captured patterns.  Refer to the *CONFIDENSE™ Module User Guide*. |
| Filtering | Opens the Point Filtering dialog box for filtering and selecting points inside the reconstruction.  See ["Point Filtering"](#target9000107). |
| Apply LAT WOI of Selected Point to All Points  Apply Voltage WOI of Selected Point to All Points | Applies the LAT/Voltage Window of Interest (WOI) of the selected point to all points and updates the map.  See ["Annotations"](#target9000108). |
| Restore WOI for All Points | Restores the original WOI to all points, and updates the map.  See ["Annotations"](#target9000109). |

### Point Menu

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/point\_menu\_6-3\_EN.xml

Table 73: Point Menu Options

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Copy Point To | Copies the selected point to the selected map, without removing it from the original map. |
| Move Point To | Moves the selected point to the selected map, removing it from the original map. |
| Delete Point | Deletes the selected point.  The point is moved to the Recycle Bin and can be restored.  See ["Deleting Points"](#target9000110). |
| Restore Point | Restores a point that is selected in the Recycle Bin.  See ["Restoring Deleted Points to the Map"](#target9000111). |
| Point Tag & Type | Opens the Point Tag dialog box for applying point (or contour) tags and changing their type.  See ["Point Tags"](#target9000112). |
| Restore Annotation | Restores the annotation of the selected point to its original value. |

### Catheter Menu

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/catheter\_menu\_7\_EN.xml

Table 74: Catheter Menu Options

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Create Snapshot | Records the locations of designated catheters at a specific time during the Study.  See ["Catheter Snapshots"](#target9000113). |
| Snapshot Settings | Controls which Catheter Snapshots are displayed. Up to six can be displayed at a time.  See ["Catheter Snapshots"](#target9000114). |
| Catheter Projection | Displays a projection of the selected catheter's tip location on the surface image. |
| Catheter Projection Settings | Sets the projected catheter. Options are mapping catheter or ablation catheter. |
| Reset Visualization | Resets the Catheter Visualization (initialization).  See ["System Status"](#target9000115). |
| Reset TPI | Resets the tissue proximity status. Move the TPI catheter to retrain.  Refer to the *CONFIDENSE™ Module User Guide*. |
| Reset Sheath Pairing | Resets pairing between the catheter and the CARTO VIZIGO™ Sheath.  Refer to the CARTO VIZIGO™*Module User Guide* . |
| Launch Catheter Definition Tool | Opens the Catheter Definition Tool, without exiting the study.  See ["Defining a Catheter with the Catheter Definition Tool"](#target9000116). |

### ECG Menu

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ECG\_menu\_6p5\_EN.xml

Table 75: ECG Menu Options

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Stimulation | Opens a list of catheters and the available intracardiac electrodes. Select the appropriate electrodes, or choose None.  See ["Selecting Stimulation Routing without Presets"](#target9000117). |
| Stimulation End Configuration | Sets the time interval used to determine the end of stimulation.  See ["End Stimulation Data"](#target9000118). |
| Stimulation Routing Presets | Shows or hides the Stimulation Routing Preset Buttons.  See ["Using the Stimulation Routing Presets"](#target9000119). |
| ICEG Unipolar Configuration | Opens the ICEG Unipolar Configuration dialog box for configuring the IVC Electrode and IVC usage.  See ["Enabling ICEG Unipolar Reference"](#target9000120) and ["Enabling ICEG Unipolar Stimulation"](#target9000121). |
| Filters Configuration | Opens the Filter Configuration dialog box for setting the high and low pass levels for each catheter.  See ["Configure ECG Filters"](#target9000122). |
| Signal Clipping | Sets the signal clipping value. |

### Display Menu

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/display\_menu\_6p5\_EN.xml

Table 76: Display Menu Options

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Set Background Color | Opens a submenu for selecting the background color. Options:   * Black (default) * Dark Gray   You can also set the background to white in the map viewers and Selected Point Viewer.  See ["Changing the Background Color"](#target9000123). |
| Anatomical Structure | Hides or shows Anatomical Structures in the active map.  Define an Anatomical Structure from the Map menu.  See ["Anatomical Structures"](#target9000124). |
| Study Data\* | Presents the study data in Review mode.  See ["Review Study Data"](#target9000125). |
| Enable Auto Zoom | When enabled, the display zooms in when switching between maps.  See ["Manually Zooming In or Out of a Map"](#target9000126). |

### Imaging Menu

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/imaging\_menu\_7\_EN.xml

For details, refer to the *CARTOMERGE™ Module User Guide*.

Table 77: Help Menu Options

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Begin CARTOMERGE™ Study | Begins a CARTOMERGE™ Module session. |
| Show Registration Toolbar | Provides quick links to most commonly used options during a CARTOMERGE™ Session. |
| Manual Registration | Provides options for fine-tuning the surface image.   * Rotate: Changes the rotation of all surface images * XY Move: Changes the position of all surface images along the XY axis * Arrow (No Tools): Stops the Rotate or XY Move option |
| Landmark Registration | Applies landmark registration |
| Surface Registration | Applies surface registration |
| Show Landmarks | Displays or hides all applied landmarks |
| Show Match Statistics | Displays the Match Statistics dialog box |
| Multi Cavity Registration | Applies to all maps/surface images |
| Undo | Undoes the previous registration-related change |
| Redo | Redoes the previous action that was undone (registration related) |
| Reset | Resets the surface image to its pre-registration/pre-alignment settings |
| Project Color | Displays projection of color on the surface image |
| Project Catheter | Displays projection of catheter tip location on the surface image |
| Project Tags | Displays projection of ABL tags on the surface image |

### Window Menu

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/menu\_window\_v6\_EN.xml

The Window menu presents a list of all available windows, such as ECG viewers and the Graphs Viewer. The available options may vary, depending on the installed software modules.

Windows that are already displayed appear in **bold**.

Click the name of a window to open it, and then drag it to the desired location.

![menu_window_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 180: Window menu

* Ultrasound: *CARTOSOUND™ Module User Guide*
* Ripple Viewer: *CONFIDENSE™ Module User Guide*

### Tools Menu

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/tools\_menu\_7\_EN.xml

Table 78: Tools Menu Options

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Preferences | Opens the Preferences dialog box.  Available tabs depend on the modules that are installed and active. |
| CFAE On | Enables the CFAE Feature.  You must acquire EA points before enabling the CFAE Feature.  Refer to the *CARTO™ CFAE Module User Guide*. |
| PASO™ Module On | Enables PASO™ Module functionality.  Refer to the *PASO™ Module User Guide*. |
| CARTOFINDER™ Module On | Enables CARTOFINDER™ Module recording.  Refer to the *CARTO PRIME™ Module User Guide*. |
| EP Recording | When working with the EP System Interface, opens a submenu with options relating to the EP Recording System.  See ["EP Recording System Interface"](#target9000132). |
| Screen Capture Parameters | Opens the Capture Preference dialog box, for setting screen capture and video preferences.  See ["Setting the Screen and Video Capture Parameters"](#target9000133). |
| Administration Tools | Opens a submenu with administrative options.   * View Log: Opens a list of available log files. * Administration Tools: Opens the password-protected Technician Tools dialog box. * Save Debugging Info: Saves details for use by Biosense Webster |
| Add Bookmark | Enables you to add a comment to the log file. |
| ACCURESP™ Module | Opens a submenu with options for the ACCURESP™ Module.  See ["ACCURESP™ Module"](#target9000134). |
| Repeater Monitor On (Monitor Display) | Activates the monitor in the operating room.  See ["Repeater Monitor Display"](#target9000135). |
| Hot Corners Settings | Opens the Hot Corners Settings dialog box, for turning the corners of the screen into user-defined shortcuts for frequently used operations.  See ["Hot Corners"](#target9000136). |
| Scar Settings | Sets the thresholds for automatically marking regions as scar tissue.  See ["Displaying Scar Areas"](#target9000137). |
| Enhanced ECG Recording | Records 10 unipolar ECG channels of the catheter connected to 20 POLE A at 8 kHz, in addition to all other recorded channels. |

### Help Menu

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/help\_menu\_6p5\_EN.xml

Table 79: Help Menu Options

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Online Help | Opens the CARTO™ 3 System Online Help. |
| Keyboard Shortcuts | Opens a list of keyboard shortcuts. |
| Product Documentation | Opens additional documentation. |
| Set ScreenTips Language | When enabled, translations of the message appear as tool tips. |
| Troubleshooting | Opens a dialog box with advanced troubleshooting features. |
| Show Visualization Matrix | Displays the catheter visualization matrix in the map viewers. |
| About CARTO™ System | Opens a panel providing information about the CARTO™ 3 System software.  See ["Viewing System Information"](#target9000138). |

# Troubleshooting

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Troubleshooting\_intro\_system\_EN.xml

Difficulties with the CARTO™ 3 System can be approached in various ways, depending on the nature of the problem.

* Users can determine the source of the difficulty based on various online messages that can appear on the main screen. Such messages could relate to:
  + Workstation (computer) and PIU communication
  + Catheter or patch connections
  + Catheter or patch location
* The System Status panel is displayed with all messages relating to system function.
* Users can follow the steps suggested herein to help determine the exact nature of the problem.

The Biosense Webster Service and Support Department can be contacted by telephone to provide assistance with any issues not covered in this chapter.

## Troubleshooting Tools

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ServiceTroubleshooting\_EN.xml

The Troubleshooting tools provide valuable information for investigating a problem or suspected problem in system functioning. When customer support staff are contacted about a service issue, they might instruct users to collect information from the Troubleshooting tools. In some cases, the collected information might help users implement a solution directly. If not, the collected information will help customer support staff prepare for an on-site service visit.

### Viewing Troubleshooting Information

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/OpenTroubleshooting\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | In the Help menu, click Troubleshooting.  The Troubleshooting window opens, displaying the Communication Status pane.  ![help_troubleshooting_simulator_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 2. | Review the communication status between the CARTO™ 3 Workstation and the following:   * PIU * DICOM network * EP Recording System * Ultrasound system   Communication status is indicated according to the key below:   | Status | Line Connecting Components | | --- | --- | | No physical connection | None | | Unsatisfactory communication | Red with ![help_troubleshooting_exclamation.png](data:image/png;base64...) | | Satisfactory communication | Green | |
| 3. | Review additional information by clicking the buttons at the bottom left of the window.  ![help_troubleshooting_simulator_all_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)   |  |  | | --- | --- | | 3.1 | Click BIT Results to review results of built-in tests.  The Power-Up BIT tab displays results of tests performed when the system was switched on. The Ongoing BIT tab displays results of the most recent ongoing test.  Pass results are indicated by ![help_troubleshooting_check_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...). Fail results are indicated by ![help_troubleshooting_minus_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...).  The elapsed time since the test is displayed on the right of the pane.  For detailed results for a specific test, click the appropriate line. | | 3.2 | Click CALi Values to review calibration values.  To update the information in this pane, click Refresh. | | 3.3 | Click Values to review values related to various sensors.  The values in this pane are updated automatically in real time. | |
| 4. | To close any pane, click its corresponding button. |
| 5. | To close the Troubleshooting window, click Close. |

## Metal Interference

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Magnetic\_distortion\_troubleshooting\_7p3\_EN.xml

If the CARTO™ 3 System detects metal interference, a System message (info, alert, or error) specifies which catheter or patch is affected, and provides troubleshooting information. If the interference affects the patches or the mapping catheter, indication appears near the Acquisition panel indicating the severity of the interference (minor, moderate, or severe). Follow the instructions in the message to resolve the problem.

Table 85: Metal Interference Indication

| Level | Indication | Message | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Minor | ![horseshoe_yellow.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Info | Mapping is possible; however, accuracy may be affected.  Use your common clinical practice for real-time verification of catheter locations throughout the procedure (inspection of IC signals, direct imaging guidance such as fluoroscopy or ultrasound, etc.). Failure to do so might result in incorrect placement of the catheter. |
| Moderate | ![horseshoe_orange.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Alert |
| Severe | ![horseshoe_red.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Error | Acquisition is disabled and the catheter visualization may disappear.  Mapping is not possible under these conditions. |
| Alert | If the affected catheter is not the mapping catheter, the message is an alert. Mapping is possible; however, accuracy may be affected. |

The metal interference detection threshold for Chest Patches can be adjusted to be less sensitive. Consult your Biosense Webster representative for details[[33]](#footnote-33).

To see current metal values related to connected catheters or patches, click the magnet icon.

![Metal_values_window_8p2.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 182: Metal Values Window

Table 86: Legend for Color Coding of Metal Values

|  | Normal  (White) | Low  (Yellow) | Suboptimal  (Orange) | Bad  (Red) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Back Patches (Patch 1,2,3)** | >0 to 5 | >5 to 8 | >8 to 25 | >25 |
| **Chest Patches (Patch 4,5,6)** | >0 to 35  or  >0 to 10 |  | >35 to 75  or  >10 to 75 | >75 |
| **Magnetic Sensor Based Catheters** | >0 to 5 | >5 to 8 | >8 to 25 | >25 |
| \* Value range when the Enhanced Sensitivity check box is selected in CAS Tools | | | | |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  For catheters with a Single Axis Sensor (SAS) such as fixed LASSO™ NAV Catheters, the metal value is displayed as N/A. |

## Electrode View

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/troubleshooting\_electrode\_view\_EN.xml

If there are issues with visualization of diagnostic catheters (for example, a spline is not visualized), you can view the unprocessed (raw) electrode positions. This shows the unprocessed positions before the visualization algorithm is applied to display the full catheter. These electrode positions can be displayed even if the catheter or spline cannot be visualized.

To view the electrode positions, press CTRL + W. Electrodes are displayed as brown discs. If electrode numbering is enabled in the Catheter Setup screen, the numbers also appear on the disc.

## Patches View

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Patches\_View\_EN.xml

During a study, the CARTO™ 3 System keeps track of changes between the initial placement of the patches and their current location.

The CARTO™ 3 System may detect a change in the position of one or more of the Back Patches or Chest Patches. This change may be due to an actual change in the position of the patch, patient movement, movement of the Location Pad, or a distortion to the magnetic field.

In **Patches View**, the System shows the distance between the initial placement of the patches and their current location.

* Current patch position is indicated by the standard green (back) and yellow (chest) discs.
* The initial patch location is indicated with striped discs. If the change in position is large, the striped discs are colored red.
* The distance between the initial patch location and the current patch location is indicated in red text next to the current patch location.

To open **Patches View**, click the background of one of the map viewers and press CTRL + P. The view appears in the selected viewer. Press CTRL + P again to hide the view.

If there is a change to the Body Coordinate System (BCS), the new patch positions are considered the initial location.

![patches_view.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 183: Patches View in the Main Map Viewer

## ECG Noise

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ECG\_Noise\_EN.xml

REP4611 D-V1-1.31.2, PL.8

To minimize ECG noise, the following guidelines should be followed.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Reducing Signal Noise | REP9626 1.9.5  ECG noise is typically generated as the result of improper connection of the body surface ECG patch to the patient. This noise is the most significant during ablation. To resolve this situation, verify proper connection. It is recommended to turn off the Notch filter for this verification. |
| Using Filters to Reduce Noise | Proper setting of the ECG filters enables ECG noise reduction. |
| Induced Noise (50 Hz and HF) | Keep all signal cables (for example, body surface cable, catheter cable, and RF generator cables) as far away as possible from AC fields (for example, Location Pad and cabling, monitors, isolation transformers, power supply, etc.). |
| Noise Generated by the Location Pad | If noise appears on the Recording System when the signals are clear on the CARTO™ 3 System, verify a proper connection between the PIU and the Recording System. If the noise continues, check if the noise is generated by the Location Pad by turning off the Location Pad. If the noise does appear to be generated by the Location Pad, contact the Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Active Right Leg (RL) | Using Unipolar ECG in a Recording System is possible only for catheters connected to the PIU. The Reference signal is either WCT or IC Reference, according to the CARTO™ 3 System configuration. |

## Communication Problems

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Communication\_Problems\_c3\_EN.xml

REP4611 PL.8b

A failure in communication between the workstation (computer) and the PIU is indicated by a message in the System Status panel that appears for more than a few seconds. If this message appears, any data acquired during the current procedure will not be lost. Once this message automatically disappears, mapping can be resumed.

## Repeater Monitor Display

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/trbl\_monitor\_c3-v4\_EN.xml

If the repeater monitor is on but there is no display, do one of the following:

* From the opening screen, click the System button and then click the Repeater Monitor On button.
* On the Tools menu, select the Repeater Monitor On option (during a CARTO™ 3 Study).

## Disconnecting the Patch Sensor Cable

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/PU\_Detaching\_service\_EN.xml

The CARTO™ 3 System supports the Patch Unit with plug-and-play functionality. Patch Unit or Patch Sensor cables can be replaced. Preserve Patch Sensor cable integrity by handling it with care.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteCaution.png](data:image/png;base64...) | *CAUTION*  Handle the Patch Sensor cable with care and avoid pulling it forcefully. Pulling the Patch Sensor cable can strain or damage the cable, connectors, or devices it is connected to. Always disconnect the Patch Sensor cable gently and in a controlled manner. |

When disconnecting the Patch Sensor cable, always pull gently from its reference housing by grasping the sensor strain relief and not by pulling on the cable.

![PU_detach_both.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 184: Patch Sensor Cable Handling

# Appendix: Clinical Data Summary

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Summary\_Clinical\_Studies\_To\_Determine\_Catheter\_Tip\_Location\_EN.xml

**Summary of Clinical Studies Conducted for Alternative Imaging To Determine Catheter Tip Location**

## Zero/Low Fluoroscopy: A nested sub-study of REAL AF Registry

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/zero-low\_fluoroscopy\_v8p1\_EN.xml

A Real-World Evidence study (REAL AF Registry Sub-Study) evaluating the safety and acute effectiveness of Paroxysmal Atrial Fibrillation ablation with a zero/low fluoroscopy workflow using contemporary radiofrequency technologies (e.g. THERMOCOOL SMARTTOUCH™, THERMOCOOL SMARTTOUCH™ SF) and electroanatomical mapping technologies (e.g. CARTO™ 3 System, CARTO VIZIGO™ Guiding Sheath, PENTARAY™ NAV eco, Webster™ CS, DECANAV™) was performed. The table below summarizes the therapeutic and diagnostic devices used in the study. The primary safety (major procedural complications) and secondary acute effectiveness (achievement of electrical PV isolation) endpoints were met in the REAL AF Registry Sub-Study in which the zero/low fluoroscopy workflow was used for left atrial ablation of paroxysmal atrial fibrillation. The results of the REAL AF Registry Sub-Study support the use of alternative imaging to fluoroscopy to aid in the manipulation and positioning of the mapping catheters/sheaths to the area of interest during a left atrial ablation procedure.
